GENERAL REQUIREMENTS


1 Scope

This specification establishes the design, performance and test
requirements for an Integrated Display System (IDS) to be installed on the
A109S helicopter equipped with P&W PW207C engine.

2 Interpretation

This specification differentiates between mandatory requirements which are
defined by the use of the term "shall" and requirements which are aims or
desirable which are defined by the use of term "should", "will" or "may".
For purposes of aiding in traceability, requirements within paragraphs are
identified with a number in square brackets preceding the period of a
sentence containing a requirement, or preceding a colon identifying a list
of requirements.

3 Purchase Order

The receipt of this specification shall not be deemed in any respect to
constitute a purchase order.

   REQUIREMENTS


1 General Description

The IDS shall monitor the engine and aircraft systems and present to the
pilot all the corresponding parameter values, as well as the caution,
warning, and advisory annunciation.

Limit exceedance data, fault data and caution and warning signals shall be
stored in a Non-Volatile Memory (NVM) for subsequent retrieval from
maintenance operator.

             The IDS shall be:


                 1. Designed to provide modular approach to aircraft system
                    integration


                 2. Software programmable through the external connector


                 3. Built with color Active Matrix Liquid Crystal Display
                    (AMLCD) technology


                 4. Fully redundant in all its input/output circuits, except
                    as specified in paras. 3.3.1.4, 3.3.1.6, and 3.3.1.7.


                 5. Fault tolerant


             The IDS shall consist of :


             (a)  Two identical and fully interchangeable cockpit mounted
                 Electronic Display Units (EDU), capable to display in
                 graphical form all the engine and aircraft information, as
                 well as failure and status annunciations.


                 The EDUs shall also be provided with a switch panel
                 featuring some dedicated and software programmable control
                 functions.


              (b) One remotely mounted Data Acquisition Unit (DAU), capable
                 to accept, condition and process all the engine and
                 aircraft sensors listed in Annexes A, and D.


                 The DAU shall be designed to be functionally redundant and
                 fault tolerant, that is, comprises two identical interface
                 and processor units, from here on called Channel A and
                 Channel B.


                 The exchange of information between the DAU and both the
                 EDUs shall be accomplished through dedicated ARINC 429 data
                 links.


The DAU shall also provide interfaces to the following external components:


        6. One Aural Warning Generator (AWG)
        7. Two Master Warning Lights (MWLs), one at each pilot's station
        8. Two Master Caution Lights (MCLs), one at each pilot's station
        9. Two engine fire detectors, one per engine
       10. One Fuel Computer Unit (FCU)
       11. One Helicopter Ground Station (HGS)


The instrument range requirements are detailed in Annex A.

Each engine is equipped with a single channel FADEC system (from here on
called Electronic Engine Control Unit - EECU), that transmits through a
digital bus the primary data to the IDS.

Analog and discrete output signals are also made available by each engine
model, in order to provide a high integrity monitoring capability.

In order to increase the IDS system integrity, the EECUs shall also be
connected to both the EDUs.

This will permit to present all the essential engine data, as  well  as  the
rotor speed parameter, even in the unlikely event of complete DAU failure.


2 Hardware Requirements


1 Performance Characteristics


1 Processor


2 CPU

The IDS shall contain a general purpose processor.

The Processor CCA contains shared RAM, Non-volatile Memory and Program
Timer are connected  to an ISA bus through an FPGA bridge (future option).

Provisions shall be made for processing capacity growth (50% at least)
(future option).


1 Memory

The IDS shall provide the following memory:

|Type                       |Size                               |
|DRAM                       |8 Mbytes (future option)           |
|NVM (Program and Data)     |1 Mbytes                           |


Main memory shall have a 50% growth capacity (future option).

NVM shall have a minimum of 10,000 write cycles.


3 Operating Time


1 Warm-up Time

The IDS shall  satisfy all performance characteristics and present
statically corrected and non-misleading information.  The following warm-up
times shall be met:


|Warm-up  Time                                       |
|Temperature      |Display          |Backlighting     |
| -40 °C          |10 minutes       |10 minutes       |
|-15 °C           |5 minutes        |10 minutes       |
|-5 °C            |                 |10 minutes       |
|0 °C             |2 minutes        |10 minutes       |
|+15 °C           |                 |5 minutes        |
|+20 °C           |5 seconds        |5 minutes        |


2 Power Interruption Time

Power interruption time shall be as specified below.

For power interruptions up to 50msec, the IDS shall continue to run without
 perceivable effect on the EDUs.

For power interruptions up to 200 msec, the IDS shall recover its
functionality and perform inflight Power-up BIT (warm start) if
WOW=inactive.

Depending on the interruption length, the EDU could be affected by quick
changes and movements on the symbology due to temporary loss of incoming
variables. The warnings, cautions and advisories (if any) may be presented
in any order.

For power interruptions longer than 200 msec, the IDS shall recover its
functionality and perform Preflight Power-up BIT (cold start). The
warnings, cautions and advisories (if any) may be presented in any order.

In no case shall the power interruptions cause steady erroneous display or
output.

3 Lag Time

The lag time between pilot selection of a format and display of the format
shall not exceed 0.5 seconds.

4 Data Update

Display data shall be updated at 7 Hz minimum rate to meet symbol motion
and interfacing sensor requirements, except during Maintenance Log display
and HGS operations, when a 4Hz
minimum rate is required  .


2 Display Characteristics


The EDU shall be provided with a color Active Matrix Liquid Crystal Display
(AMLCD) in accordance with the following requirements:




             |Item           |Requirement              |
|Active area    |112.3mm (W) x 83.5mm (H) |
|Resolution     |320 (W) x 234 (H) pixels |
|               |(960 sub-pixels on the   |
|               |horizontal axis)         |
|Sub-pixel pitch|0.119 mm (W) x 0.357 mm  |
|               |(H)                      |
|Pixel          |RGB vertical stripes     |
|configuration  |                         |
|Viewing        |-45° to +45° (horizontal |
|envelope       |axis)                    |
|               |-10° to +30° (vertical   |
|               |axis)                    |



             1 Display Visual Characteristics


1 Response Time

Response time limitation shall not produce undesirable artifacts which
could lead to erroneous interpretation or loss of displayed information.
Such artifacts include smearing of moving images and loss of luminance.
These effects may be temperature dependent and shall be evaluated over the
operating temperature range.

For either increasing or decreasing commanded luminance, the ratio of
integrated luminance change (i.e. the luminance perceived by the eye) to
commanded steady state luminance shall be greater than 70%.  The perceived
luminance change is mathematically defined in ARP 4256.

2 Symbol Quality

Symbols shall not have distracting gaps or geometric distortions which
cause erroneous interpretations; any distorted dimension shall not exceed
one local line width.

3 Symbol Alignment

Symbols which are interpreted relative to each other (i.e. cursors on
scales, etc.) shall be aligned to preclude misinterpretation of
information.

4 Symbol Motion

Display symbology that is in motion (translation and/or rotation) shall not
have distinct jitter, jerkiness or ratcheting effects.  Dynamic symbols
shall maintain luminance, contrast, color, line width and symbol quality
characteristics independent of their rate of motion.

Pointers and bar graphs shall be designed with built in hysteresis or
smoothing of the displayed value, such that when a constant or near
constant value of a parameter is to be displayed, objectionable motion is
eliminated.

5 Matrix Anomalies

Since the display is an array of discrete elements, displayed information
may have visible spatial and color anomalies.  Stair stepping, line width
variation and moiré are examples of spatial anomalies, while color banding
and color fringing are examples of color anomalies.  Anomalies are
especially visible in dynamic images and may not be visible in static
images.  The extent of the anomaly is dependent on many factors including
size, shape and arrangement of the display elements, construction of the
symbol, rate, direction and increment of motion and luminance control of
the elements.

The display shall minimize matrix anomalies which cause distraction or
erroneous interpretation.  This shall be assessed with both dynamic and
static images.

6 Jitter

There shall be no discernible jitter when viewed within the viewing
envelope.  Jitter of 0.3 mrad. peak-to-peak from any point within the
viewing envelope is an upper limit, but may not be acceptable in some
instances.  Therefore, jitter shall not be greater than one local line
width.

7 Line Width Uniformity

When viewed from any point within the viewing envelope, lines of a
specified color and luminance should appear uniform in width at all
rotational or translational orientations of the line.  Line width
variations should not be readily apparent or cause distracting effects.
Unless otherwise specified, the minimum width of a line shall not be less
than 70% of its maximum width, in order to avoid any visual "roping"
effect.

8 Flicker

Flicker shall not be readily discernible or distracting under day,
twilight, or night conditions, considering both foveal and full peripheral
vision, and using an operational format most susceptible to producing
flicker.

This is intended to include stroboscopic flicker induced by vibration
and/or pilot motion.  Therefore, the minimum display refresh rate shall be
60 Hz.

9 Image Retention

Image retention is an undesired afterimage that persists on the display.
Therefore, image retention shall not be readily discernible day or night
and shall not be distracting or cause an erroneous interpretation of the
display.

10 Multiple Images

When illuminated with light normal to the display surface, for example sun
- shafting illumination, transflective LCD displays can produce multiple
images of displayed information.  The multiple images formed shall neither
be distracting nor cause erroneous interpretation of displayed data.


11 Defects

The number of acceptable element failures is an aesthetic issue.   Failed-
OFF and failed-ON blue elements are much less objectionable than failed-ON
red, green, or white elements.  Clusters of failed elements are more
objectionable that those widely separated.  Visible defects on the display
surface (such as failed-ON or failed-OFF elements, rows or columns, spots,
discolored areas, etc.) shall not be distracting or cause an erroneous
interpretation of the display.  A color pixel shall be considered as failed
as soon as one of its color dots is failed.
Defects which are not visible with any operational format from the minimum
viewing distance are acceptable.  There shall be no more than 0.03% failed
color pixels. Color pixel shall be
considered as failed when one of its color dots is failed.:

- Clusters shall not be closer than 30 mm from each other.
- Elementary defects shall not be closer than 10 mm from each other.

The number of acceptable defects is dependent on the display format;
however, the number of failed dots should be as specified below.


|Display  Defects                                |
|Cluster  Size          |Acceptable  Number     |
|                       |of  Clusters           |
|2 dots                 |(11                    |
|3 ( n ( 5 dots         |(7                     |
|6 ( n ( 10 dots        |(4                     |
|11 ( n ( 22 dots       |(2                     |
|n > 22 dots            |No clusters            |


Design goals for clusters shall be:

No cluster consisting of more than 5 defective dots
Defective dots should be OFF

1 Failed-ON Row/Columns

No failed-ON row/columns shall be permitted on the display.


2 Failed-OFF Row/Columns

Depending on resolution, mode, color and format, there may be failed-OFF
row/column defects which are neither distracting nor cause erroneous
interpretation.  In no case shall a failed-OFF row/column cause any
erroneous interpretation.  If a failed-OFF row/column is in an unused area
(format dependent) or is orthogonal to symbol lines, it will be visually
detected by the operator during the IDS maintenance test.


3 Defect Service Limits

Defects should not constitute a service limit unless they are distracting
or could cause an erroneous interpretation.  Even though failed-ON
row/columns are not acceptable at testing, they might be tolerated in
service for a time if safe flight is not threatened by distraction or
erroneous interpretation.  Dispatch capability can and should be enhanced
by designing the display hardware/software system to be as tolerant of
failures as practical; an example is designing symbols with displayed lines
so that no single failed row/column can cause an erroneous interpretation.

12 Cross-talk

Cross-talk problems shall be held to a minimum; more precisely, the
variation induced shall be less than a single gray level.

2 Photo- Colorimetric Characteristics


1 General

The display symbology shall be clearly readable throughout the viewing
envelope under all ambient illumination levels ranging from 1.1 lux (0.1
fc) to sun-shining illumination of 100,000 lux (9,300 fc) incidence on the
face of the display.

2 Luminance


1 Maximum Luminance

With manual and automatic luminance controls at maximum, the average
luminance shall be as follows.


|Color           |Luminance    |
|White           |343 cd/m2    |
|                |min.         |
|Red             |85 cd/m2 min.|
|Green           |210 cd/m2    |
|                |min.         |
|Blue            |35 cd/m2 min.|
|Black           |10 cd/m2 min.|
|(background)    |             |

The above luminance requirements are based on a 0.119 mm line width for
colors and on the average display area for black.  Larger line widths and
filled areas will require luminance to provide the same apparent
brightness.  Conversely, smaller line widths will require more luminance
for the same apparent brightness.  Other symbol colors typically have a
luminance level significantly lower than white.  The actual level of the
luminance disparity between white and other colors is dependent on several
factors, including pixel configuration, phosphor mix, and color filters
employed.

Other colors, especially red and blue hues, have greater perceived
brightness than white, which partially compensates for this luminance
disparity.  The use of larger line widths, symbol area fills, and white
outlining of dimmer symbol colors should be used to further compensate for
lower symbol color luminance values.

2 Minimum Luminance

To achieve acceptable viewing in dark conditions, the minimum white symbol
luminance shall be no greater than 0.343 cd/m² (1 fl) for 0.119 mm line
width.  Under night flying with the display brightness set at the lowest
usable level for flight with normal symbology, all flags and annunciators
shall be adequately visible.  The symbology shall be dimmed homogeneously,
both in color and luminance.

3 Luminance Uniformity

Display area luminance shall not vary more than 0.776 normal to the display
surface and with the display set to its maximum gray scale (full-on).

      Luminance uniformity = (Lmax - Lmin)/Lmean

Where:

Lmax: Maximum area luminance of a specified area measured from any eye
position within the viewing envelope

Lmin: Minimum area luminance of a specified area  measured from any eye
position within the viewing envelope

Lmean: Mean area luminance of a specified area measured from any eye
position within the viewing envelope


4 Background (Black) Uniformity

The total difference in the color at any two locations commanded to their
dark state shall not exceed 12 when measured under standard conditions.

These limits apply for any fixed eye position within the viewing envelope
with the display integral lighting set to full intensity.
Background uniformity is mathematically defined in ARP 4256, par. 5.2.2.5.

5 Background (Black) Chroma

The average background chroma measured across the display surface shall be
less than or equal to 26 anywhere within the viewing envelope when measured
with the display integral lighting set to full intensity.

Background chroma is mathematically defined in ARP 4256, par. 5.2.2.6.


3 Contrast Ratio

The contrast ratio (total foreground luminance/total background luminance)
shall be sufficient to provide a promptly discernible, easy to read image
under all conditions of cockpit illumination and under all conditions of
eye adaptation to the external visual scene.

Image brightness, the subjective perception of luminance, is heavily
dependent on image size or line width.

Smaller line widths than those referenced in the following paragraphs will
require higher contrast ratios for comparable readability.

1 Dark Ambient Contrast Ratio

The average white contrast ratio over the usable display surface shall be a
minimum of 20:1 on the normal viewing axis and 10:1 for any eye position
within the viewing envelope.

This requirement is based on a 0.35 mm line width.

For line widths of 1 mm or greater, the average white contrast ratio on the
normal viewing axis shall be a minimum of 15:1.

2 High Ambient Contrast Ratio

The average white contrast ratio over the usable display surface shall be a
minimum of 3:1 for 0.5 mm line widths and 2:1 for 1 mm line widths, when
measured from any eye position within the viewing envelope.

This requirement shall apply when the display is subject to point source
illumination level of 100,000 lux at 45° of incidence.

The contrast values for red, green and blue colors shall be as specified
below.

|Color            |Contrast         |
|Red              |1.3 min.         |
|Green            |2.0 min.         |
|Blue             |1.0 min.         |


3 Isocontrast Curves

The VENDOR should provide a set of isocontrast curves defined for the white
color in the following three levels of illumination at 45° incidence:
10,000, 50,000 and 100,000 lux.

The isocontrast diagrams should be presented for, at least, the following
contrast values: 5:1, 10:1, 15:1 and 20:1.


4 Colors

The color chromaticity with the white backlighting shall be as follows
(ref. CIE 1976 UCS).

|Color     |u'        |v'        |r         |
|Red       |0.393     |0.531     |0.020     |
|Green     |0.131     |0.565     |0.020     |
|Blue      |0.121     |0.361     |0.020     |




                     2 Chroma Uniformity

A symbol of a specified color set to any intensity within the specified
dimming range shall have a chroma which is uniform across the usable
display area when viewed from any eye position.

Moreover, the intended colors shall be identifiable in the presence or
absence of other colors and distinguishable from each other.
Chroma uniformity is mathematically defined in ARP 4256. par. 5.2.4.1.


3 Chroma Tolerance

Within viewing envelope, the intended colors shall be identifiable in the
presence or absence of other colors and distinguishable from each other.

All displayed image colors measured at the display center from any point
within the viewing envelope shall have a chroma difference not greater than
26 when measured in accordance with ARP 4256, par. 5.2.4.3.


5 Gray Scale

Gray scale control may be required to match chromaticity requirements,
remove stair stepping, render video images or provide luminance control.
The degree of control needed depends on the purpose.

When gray scale is used for anti-aliasing, deviations in luminance levels
can lead to variations in line shape and legibility.

When gray scale is used for color selection, deviations in primary color
gray scale luminance levels can lead to variations in chromaticity.

Gray scale luminance level deviations throughout the viewing envelope
should be minimized to preclude misleading information or distraction.


6 Specular Reflections

The display front surface shall have a reflection reducing coating with
performance characteristics in accordance with MIL-C-14806.

Electrode patterns and other non emitting areas shall have low reflectivity
coatings to minimize internal reflections.

In no case shall the level of reflectivity and resulting loss of contrast
be sufficient to cause distracting or erroneous interpretation.


3 Lighting Requirements


1 AMLCD Display

The AMLCD display shall be backlighted with at least two redundant
fluorescent lamps, whose luminance shall be manually adjusted through a
suitable control and automatically through two Ambient Light Sensor (ALS),
both installed on the display bezel (future option).

A cockpit mounted Day/Night switch shall be used to automatically command
the dimming of display luminance down to 51 cd/m2  (15fl)
.

2 Luminance Control

The display dimmer shall be capable of starting and operating the lamps
under all conditions of temperature.
A calibrated light intensity sensor shall measure the average intensity of
the lamps and shall be used as a reference to modify the high voltage
supply output due to temperature variation, phosphor depreciation, and lamp
replacement are compensated for automatically.
The internal control shall employ an exponential filter response to
automatic dimming changes in brightness.
The rise for brightness increase shall be 1 second and decay time for
brightness decrease shall be <60 seconds.

3 Heater Control

The internal temperature of the EDU, in the area of the AMLCD and lamps,
shall be monitored and regulated by its electronics.  External temperatures
below the normal operation levels of the lamp or AMLCD shall activate a
heating element to raise the temperature to provide normal display
operation.  Once operation is achieved, the heater shall be turned off.

Protection against failures in the heater control circuitry should be
provided by a safety thermostat, which opens the heater circuit if the
internal temperature setting is exceeded.


4 Dim Control

The AMLCD luminance shall occur in accordance with the following
requirements:

a) In the range 10 Lux to 10,000 Lux, the display luminance shall be
automatically controlled by the ALS, while the manual control shall allow
the operator to modify the luminance around the nominal value given by the
ALS.

b) In the range 10,000 Lux to 100,000 Lux, the manual control shall allow
the operator to increase the display luminance up to the maximum luminance.

A luminance increase commanded by the ALS shall be rapid (less than 1 sec),
while a luminance decrease shall be slow (less than 60 sec). (future
option).
Failure to the ALS shall not affect the operation of manual control.

5 Night Mode

When the night mode is selected, the following shall occur:

a) The display luminance shall be manually adjustable using the rocker
  switch, in the range 0.34 cd/m2  (0.1 fl) to 51 cd/m2  (15fl).

b) The DAU shall reduce the output voltage to the external lamps down to 14
  VDC approx., in order to achieve the following luminance values:

   . MWL    51 cd/m2  (15fl)
   . MCL    51 cd/m2  (15fl)

For CAA certification, means shall be provided to undim  the MWLs and the
MCLs when the Night mode is selected.


4 Bezel Lighting

The bezel and function keys lighting shall be 5 VDC type, NVG Green A
color.


5 Night Vision Goggles (NVG) Compatibility

When required the NVG compatibility shall be in accordance with the
requirements of Agusta specification 100-70-68 Type1, Class B.

The NVG shall be achieved through the adoption of a filter.

Attachment details shall be defined by the vendor and approved by Agusta.

The detailed requirements are specified below.

a)    Colors

       AMLCD Display:

|Color Chromaticity  (CIE 1976 UCS)              |
|Color      |u'         |v'         |R          |
|Red        |0.390      |0.532      |0.020      |
|Green      |0.142      |0.566      |0.020      |
|Blue       |0.140      |0.333      |0.020      |


   b) NVG Radiance (NR)

|Switch  Panel       |AMLCD  Display      |
|1.7 x 10-10 NRa @   |2.5 x 10-9 NRb @ 0.5|
|0.1 FL.             |FL.                 |
|                    |(white color)       |
|                    |2.4 x 10-6 NRb @ 0.5|
|                    |FL.                 |
|                    |(max)               |

   c) Luminance

|Switch  Panel            |AMLCD  Display  |
|0 to 5.1 cd/m² (1.5 FL)  |  0.34 cd/m²    |
|                         |(0.1 FL)        |
|                         |to 51  cd/m² (15|
|                         |FL)             |


3 Electrical Characteristics


1 Power Supply

The IDS shall normally operate from the helicopter 28 VDC power
distribution system and from the battery in the event of failure to the
generator.

The power consumption shall be as follows:


             (a)  EDU


            12. 11 Watts at 28 VDC for display electronics
            13. 20 Watts at 28 VDC for display backlighting
            14. 31 Watts at 28 VDC for AMLCD heaters (1)
            15. 40 Watts at 28 VDC for backlighting heaters (2)
            16. 4.2 Watts at 5 VDC for switch panel lighting




Notes:

(1) The AMLCD heaters shall only be active when the ambient temperature is
                     less than 0 °C.


(2) The display lighting heater shall only be active when the ambient
   temperature is less than 25   °C.


 The typical EDU power consumption during normal operation shall be 31
 Watts at 28 VDC (electronics and display lighting active).


             (b)  DAU


15 Watts at 28 VDC per channel, 30 Watts total

2 Bonding

 The bonding requirements shall be in accordance with MIL-B-5087B, Class R,
       as follows:


                (a)  EDU


                An electrical bonding surface per MIL-C-5541 Class 3 shall
                be provided at the EDU bezel surface to allow direct metal
                to metal contact.


                (b)  DAU


                An electrical bonding surface per MIL-C-5541 Class 3, with a
                threaded hole in the center, shall be provided on the front
                panel of the DAU to allow direct contact via braided cable.



                4 Physical Characteristics


1 Dimensions and Weight

Figure 8 shows the EDU outline dimensions.


Figure 7 shows the DAU outline dimensions (3 MCU in accordance with ARINC
600). The fixing to the helicopter frame shall be through a mounting tray
(not covered by this specification).


Unit weight shall be as follows:


            17. EDU: 2.2 Kg max.
            18. DAU: 3.8 Kg max.
            19.

2 Connectors

The EDU connector shall comply with MIL - C - 38999 ,series 3.

The DAU connector shall comply with Type ARINC 600 shell size 2.


3 Cooling

The IDS system shall be cooled using simple convection or conduction
through mounting structure when installed on the instrument panel.  No
forced air cooling shall be for IDS required but can be provided if the
cockpit definition requires air movement.

5 Interfaces


1 Input/Output Signals

The electrical characteristics of input/output signals are specified in
Annex D to this specification.  The specified interfaces represent the
system requirements for the intended specifications, including growth
potential.

1 Input Signal Conditioning

The IDS system shall provide the necessary signal buffering, isolation, and
filtering to the engine and aircraft sensor.

All the discrete inputs shall be filtered with 0.2 sec. delay, except the
MWL reset, MCL reset and Master reset inputs.

Each channel shall be sufficiently isolated such that a failure in one
channel will not cause a failure in the other channel.

Differential input devices shall be used into the DAU and the total input
impedance shall be at least as follows:

TOT signals > 300 KOhms
RTD signals > 1 KOhms
Other signals    > 100 KOhms

The input terminals shall be isolated from aircraft ground by at least 200
KOhms.

2 Output Signals Protection

All the DAU output signals that are required to be connected in parallel
shall be diode isolated and/or protected against short circuits, as
applicable.


3 Data Conversion and Accuracy

Data conversion of each analog signal to a digital format shall have a
resolution of 0.025% and a minimum data sampling rate consistent with the
requirements of this specification.

The total accuracy for each parameter shall be at least as follows:


                 (a)  RPM signals


                 ±0.1% F.S., over the range 50% to 120%
                 ±0.2% F.S., elsewhere


                 (b)  TOT signals


                 ±4 °C over the range 500 °C to 1020 °C
                 ±5 °C elsewhere




                 (c)  Torque signals


                 ±1% F.S. in normal mode (EECU healthy)
                 ±7% based on 100% = 394 lb-ft in back-up mode (EECU faulty)


                 (d)  Other input signals


                 ±1.5% F.S.


The above accuracies shall apply over the temperature range from 0 °C to
+50 °C and do not include the accuracy of the external sensors.

For temperature ranges from -40 °C to 0 °C and from +50 °C to +70 °C the
above accuracies shall be multiplied by 1.5.

6 Safety Requirements


1 Qualitative Requirements

The following qualitative requirements shall apply, as applicable:

Adequate provisions and markings for lifting and handling the equipment
shall be provided.

The equipment shall be designed to minimize release of parts in the event
of crash.

1 Personnel Safety

The IDS shall be designed and manufactured to prevent injury to the
personnel during the installation, operation, maintenance, and repair or
interchanging of the complete system, equipment assembly or component parts
thereof.

2 Hazard Protection

Sources which could be hazardous to maintenance personnel (e.g. high
voltage sources capable of producing shock hazards) shall be fully
protected and adequately indicated.

3 Electrical Protection

The IDS design shall incorporate methods to protect personnel from
accidental contact with high voltages in excess of 30 VDC during normal
operation or in presence of failures.

4 Incorrect Assembly

The IDS shall be designed to avoid incorrect assembly or replacement which
would result in damage, malfunction, or reduction of safety.

5 System Failures

The IDS shall be designed to prevent the occurrence of cascade failures
within the system, or which may affect systems/equipment interfacing with
the IDS.

6 Toxic Fumes

The material or components used in the equipment manufacture shall not
liberate gases which combine with atmosphere to form an acid or corrosive
alkali, nor shall they liberate toxic or corrosive fumes which could be
hazardous to crew or maintenance personnel.


2 Quantitative Requirements

No single hardware failure shall result in a functional failure classified
as HAZARDOUS (refer to the Functional Failure Criticality table).

|               |of any caution    |         |            |                 |
|               |indication.       |         |            |                 |

The Supplier is required to determine if other functional failures are to
be considered.

1 Quantitative Requirements



     a) The probability of occurrence of a hardware related functional
        failure classified as HAZARDOUS shall be less than:

                           1( 10-8 per flight hour

     b) The probability of occurrence of a hardware related functional
        failure classified as MAJOR shall be less than:

                           1( 10-6 per flight hour

     c) Where software faults, in combination with one or more hardware
        faults, can result in a functional failure classified as HAZARDOUS,
        the probability of the hardware fault(s) shall be less than:

                           1( 10-6 per flight hour


2 Tasks to be undertaken by the Supplier

Compliance with the above safety qualitative and quantitative requirements
shall be demonstrated by analysis. The analysis shall also consider the
following, as applicable:

     1. Common cause failures
     2. Cascade failures
     3. Dormant failures
     4. HIRF and lightning (direct and indirect) effects
     5. Environmental effects

Documents to be delivered are as follows:

     1. Failure Mode, Effects and Criticality Analysis (FMECA) to be
        performed according to MIL-STD-1629A and to include the qualitative
        safety requirements, as applicable, identified in previous
        paragraphs.

     2. Fault Tree Analysis for any of the above functional failures.

The above documentation shall be delivered within three months of receipt
of this specification. In the event of design changes following the receipt
of the equipment order, the Supplier shall submit revised documentation
within two months of the change being agreed.


3 Functional Requirements



1 System Requirements



1 DAU

The DAU shall be configured in a "hot-standby" mode; that is, Channel A
shall always be the active channel (default mode), while Channel B shall be
the standby one.

Should Channel A fail, an automatic change-over to Channel B shall occur
without loosing the display of information.

The DAU shall perform the following:

   20. Monitor the information provided by the EECUs and by all the analog
       and discrete sensors and transmit it to both the EDUs, as
       appropriate.


       If any EECU data is declared invalid , then the information provided
       by the corresponding engine analog sensor or by the specific
       algorithm (see Annex A) shall be automatically made available for
       display purpose


   21. Activate the MWLs and/or the MCLs (as appropriate) in the event of
       any warning and/or caution condition, respectively.


   22. Activate the AWG (as appropriate) to present the specified aural
       annunciation.


   23. Store all the failures and exceedances in a NVM for maintenance
       purpose.


       The data downloading/uploading shall be accomplished through the HGS
       port.


       In the event of a dual Channel failure, both the EDUs shall display
       the Cruise mode, as only the EECUs information is made available.
       An IDS caution annunciation shall be displayed to alert the pilot in
       case of single or dual channel failure.



       2 IDS Controls

Each EDU shall be provided with the following controls placed on the
display bezel:

      (a)  One ON/OFF rocker switch. When the switch of one EDU is set to
         OFF the reversionary mode shall be commanded on the other EDU.


      (b)  One DIM/BRT (BRT for brite) rocker switch for manual adjustment
         of display  luminance.


      (c)  Five momentary pushbuttons (soft keys) for selection  of  display
         formats and data entry.

      (d)  One rocker switch with vertical arrows for scroll function of
         caution, warning and advisory annunciations, as well as for menu
         options.

      (e)  One ENT/CLR (ENT for enter / CLR for clear) rocker switch for
         clearing of status annunciations and for data entry functions.

After the initialization process is completed, the operator may choose any
of the following primary functions/modes, depending upon the prevailing
operational conditions and needs as identified in the following paragraphs.
 Specific bezel button controls will be defined to apply to each display
format, as detailed in following paragraphs.

       START      This key shall be used to select the START mode on EDU 1.

       CRUISE     This key shall be used to select the CRUISE mode on EDU 1.


       MAIN This key shall be used to select the MAIN mode on EDU 2.


       AUX  This key shall be used to select the AUX (auxiliary) mode (i.e.
                      hydraulic, electrical and fuel information) on EDU 2.


       TEST This key shall be used to initiate the BIT of IDS system.
                      It can be operated only when the helicopter is on
                               ground
                      (WOW = active).


       POWER CHECK     This key shall be used to initiate the Power
                      Assurance Check recording.


       EXIT This key shall be used to escape from the menu selection and to
                      page back to the primary mode of operation.


       DAU (CH-A) This key shall be used to manually select the DAU Channel
                      A as primary source. At initial power-up, the legend
                      CH-A shall be displayed in green to indicate that
                      this is the active channel. Once selected, a white
                      box shall surround the green legend.
                      The channel failure shall be indicated in yellow.


       Note:  During normal operation, the EECUs are the primary sources for
            the EDUs for engine and rotor data, while DAU CH-A is the
            primary source for secondary data and most of the cautions and
            warnings.


       DAU (CH-B) This key shall be used to manually select the DAU Channel
                      B as primary source. At initial power-up, the legend
                      CH-B shall be displayed in white to indicate that
                      this is the hot-standby channel. Once selected, a
                      white box shall surround the green legend.
            The channel failure shall be indicated in yellow.


       DATA This key shall be used to access the SENSORS DATA page
                      containing information and status regarding each
                      individual strain-gauge sensor and collective pitch
                      position sensor interfaced with the DAU. It can be
                      operated only when the helicopter is on ground (WOW =
                      active).This page shall permit the ability to
                      initiate the calibration (CAL) sequence of all strain-
                      gauge sensors.


       MAINT TEST This key shall be used to perform an extensive EDU screen
                      test, as well as to access the IDS STATUS page.
                      It can be operated only when the helicopter is on
                      ground
                      (WOW = active).


       RTN  This key shall be used to page back or Return (RTN) to the
                      previous mode of operation.


       When the Maintenance (MAINT) menu is accessed, the following modes
       shall be provided:


       FAULT      This key shall be used to access the Fault Log.


       CUM  This key shall be used to access the Cumulative Log.


       EXCEED     This key shall be used to access the Exceedance Log.


       PWR  This key shall be used to access the Power Assurance Log.


       SWAP
       (Future Option) This key shall be used to swap the display formats
                      between the two EDUs; that is, the CRUISE format
                      shall be presented on EDU 2 and the MAIN format on
                      EDU 1 (to be used for pilot training).The OEI
                      Training mode can also be presented on EDU 2, if
                      selected. To return to the normal operating
                      condition, press the SWAP key again to deactivate the
                      selection. The SWAP key shall be inactive if an OEI
                      or Autorotation condition exists.

       COPY
       (Future Option) This key shall be used to copy the CRUISE format on
                      EDU 2 (to be used for pilot training).The OEI
                      Training mode can also be presented on EDU 2, if
                      selected. To return to the normal operating
                      condition, press the SWAP key on EDU 2.
                      The COPY key shall be inactive if an OEI or
                      Autorotation condition exists.



                      3 Maintenance Port

The DAU shall provide, through the main connector, an RS-232C port  to
allow connection to a helicopter ground station (HGS) for maintenance
purposes.
A maintenance interlock signal in the form of a wire jumper shall be
provided in order to detect the DAU removal from the helicopter (future
option).

The IDS software associated to the Maintenance function shall be designed
and partitioned in such a way that the downloading/uploading through the
Maintenance port shall not corrupt the software modules related to the
basic IDS operation (i.e. display symbology, data constants, control and
moding functions, etc.).


Moreover, the software interface which provides the access to the contents
of Data Logs for downloading/uploading purposes shall be always transparent
with respect to the possible software release upgrades that may occur
throughout the entire life cycle of the same unit part number.
In other words, the access to the contents of Data Log shall be organized
by file names (e.g Fault, Exceedance, Cumulative, etc.) and not by direct
reference to memory address in the DAU.

The downloading through the Maintenance port shall be possible only through
an access protected routine on the HGS. The HGS consist of: Software to be
installed on a Laptop,
(including an Operating Manual/Help screen function) and an interface cable
between Maintenance Port and Laptop.

4 Engine Bleed Air Valve Control

Each DAU Channel shall provide a relay driver output signal to disable the
operation of engine bleed air valve (one per engine).
In case of detected ENG OUT condition, the DAU shall enable the output
signal to the bleed valve circuit of affected engine (bleed valve closed).
In case of detected ENG FIRE condition, the DAU shall enable both the
output signals to avoid smoke ingestion in the cockpit area.
In both cases, the output signals shall be disabled again when the failure
condition is cleared, otherwise they shall remain active.
Each output signal shall be a +28 VDC/open relay driver.

5 Flight/Ground State

The two EDUs and the DAU shall provide an interface to the WOW discrete
switch to determine the ground/flight condition.
The WOW input signal shall be a ground/open type (open = flight).
In order to provide a safety logic in case of any malfunction to the WOW
signal (i.e. switch stuck in flight), the IDS shall also use the NR
parameter to discriminate between ground and flight condition.
 The flight condition shall, therefore, be established when:

            24. WOW switch = open, or
            25. NR > 80 ( 1 %


In case of loss of any of the above primary signals, the corresponding back-
up signal shall be used  for  the  determination  of  flight  condition,  if
available and valid.

6 Fire Detector Test Function

Each DAU Channel shall contain a double-pole-double-throw (DPDT) relay for
testing of engine fire detector (one per engine).

All the relay contacts shall be made available on the external connectors.

When the operator initiates the IDS test function, the DAU shall enable all
the relays for simultaneous testing of both engine fire detectors.

During the test sequence the external ENG FIRE warning lights and the AWG
will be activated by the fire detectors and the relevant warning
annunciation will also be displayed on EDU 1.

The IDS shall continuously monitor the status of fire detector output
signals (+28 VDC/open type) and display a FIRE DET caution annunciation if
no signal is provided by any fire detector.

The caution annunciation shall be recorded in the Fault Log and kept on  EDU
1 until next IDS power cycle.

7 Fuel Computer Unit (FCU) Test Function

Each DAU Channel shall contain a double-pole-double-throw (DPDT) relay  for
testing of FCU functions, comprising a fuel low sensor (Switch closure SWCL
type) and a fuel quantity indicating channel for each fuel tank.

All the relay contacts shall be made available on the external connectors.

When the operator initiates the IDS test function, the DAU shall enable all
the relays for simultaneous testing of all FCU functions.

The IDS shall monitor the status of fuel low output signals (ground/open
type) and display a
F LOW FAIL caution annunciation if no signal is provided by either sensor
when the test is in progress.

The caution annunciation shall be kept on EDU 1 until the next IDS power
cycle.

Furthermore, during the test sequence the FCU analog output signals shall
cause the fuel quantity digital displays to read 0 Kg (2 mA input signal).

FCU Failure Indication :
When the analog output signal of either FCU channel is less than 1.93 mA,
the affected fuel quantity readout shall display a white box with 3 red
dashes.


8 Power Lever Angle (PLA) Monitoring

The DAU shall continuously monitor the flight idle position of each PLA and
display a caution annunciation when a ground signal is received from the
relevant discrete switch installed on the PLA quadrant.

The caution annunciation shall not be displayed when any, or a combination,
of the following conditions for the relevant engine is detected by the DAU:

            26. EECU failure
            27. EECU in Manual Mode
            28. Engine Out

The caution annunciation shall not be recorded in the Fault Log.

9 Date/Time Clock

The DAU shall be provided with a real time clock (RTC) that will continue
to run even if the main power supply is turned off.

The RTC shall be powered by a lithium battery, whose life shall be of at
least 5 years.

The RTC shall provide data in seconds, minutes, hours, day, date, month and
year and shall feature a resolution of at least 1 second.

The accuracy of the RTC shall be as follows:

     29. 1.0 minute/month over 0 to 45 °C
     30. 3.0 minutes/month over -20 to 70 °C
     31. 7.5 minutes/month over -40 to 70 °C


If the battery voltage is low, (3.125 Vdc +/-5%) then the MAINTENANCE
annunciation shall be displayed (WOW = active) and the failure recorded as
DAU BATT LOW in the Fault Log.

10 Encoding Altimeter (Alticoder) Monitoring

The DAU shall provide a provision for interfacing with a remote Alticoder
complying with
ICAO Annex 10 (future option).


11 Chip Detectors

The DAU shall provide interfacing for the following:


             |A109S                        |
|2 chip detectors per engine  |
|(1)                          |
|2 main transmission chip     |
|detectors                    |
|1 tail gearbox chip detector |



and the EDU shall display the corresponding  caution  annunciation  (Boolean
OR logic) as listed in Annex D.

Each chip detector input signal shall be recorded in the Fault Log and
             Cumulative Log.


Note:
(1)  A 100 Kohms series resistor is provided on the input circuit when the
chip detector is inactive.

12 Chip Burning

The DAU shall provide an interface to the optional Tedeco "Zapper" chip
burner system
(DAU connector B pin D13) and count the number (nnnnn) of chip detectors
burner system activation injected into the chip detector in the cumulative
Log.
A 28VDC/open type signal (28VDC = active) will be provided to the DAU by
the "fuzz"
push-button when it is pressed.

13 Comparison Monitoring Function

A comparison monitoring function shall be implemented into the IDS in order
to determine any possible malfunction and/or display of misleading
information.
Therefore, each EDU shall perform a cross-monitoring between the digital
data provided by the two DAU channels and verify their integrity as
follows:

(a)   Primary Data

RPM   >1%
TOT   >20 °C
TRQ   Not compared

The following thresholds shall apply in the performance of signal
monitoring:

N1    >5%
NR/N2 >20%
TOT   >50 °C
TRQ   Not applicable

Additional loss of signal monitoring shall also be added to the DAU
frequency inputs, such that if either N1, N2, or NR signal is absent for 5
sec. or more while EECU data for that parameter is available and valid, "CH-
A" and/or "CH-B" legends shall appear in yellow in MENU 2 sub-page of EDUs,
and 3 red dashes shall also appear in the affected parameter digital box
(FAIL legend for N2) when the appropriate DAU channel is manually selected.

Additional loss of signal monitoring shall also be added to the DAU
frequency inputs, such that if either N1, N2, or NR signal are absent for a
time greater than 1 sec, "CH-A" and/or "CH-B" legends shall appear in
yellow in MENU 2 sub-page of EDUs, and 3 red dashes shall also appear in
the affected parameter digital box (Fail legend for N2) when the
appropriate DAU channel is manually selected.

Once declared as loss of signal, the signal shall have to match for at
least 3 sec. before it is considered good again.



(b)   Secondary data (future option):

Any parameter    >6% F.S.

The data comparison shall be based on 10 sec. averaging period and shall
not be executed in case of loss of either DAU channel. If any parameter is
declared invalid, the comparison for that particular parameter shall not be
executed.

If the monitored data miscompare (MISCMP), a DAU MISCMP-P caution message
for primary data (see case (a.)  above) and DAU MISCMP-S caution message
for secondary data (see case (b) above) shall be displayed to inform the
pilot that a discrepancy exists between the two DAU channels.

The IDS shall be configured in such a manner that any single failure in the
processing channel shall not affect the display of information, as
specified in 3.3.4.

The comparison monitoring function shall not adversely affect the display
of information in case of discrepancy, that is, the display of any
parameter and/or data shall always be provided along with the miscompare
annunciation.

2 System Configuration

The DAU and EDUs shall be provided with the following aircraft
configuration signals. These settings will be verified as part of Preflight
Power-up BIT (Para 3.3.6.1.1).

the generator capacity option, as specified below:

  the fail sensor.

when the power switch is activated and EDU 1 shall display the yellow PITOT

3 Data Recording

The IDS shall be capable of logging the following type of data

    . Set-up data
    . Faults
    . Exceedances
    . Cumulative data
    . Time histories
    . Power Assurance Check Data
    . Status

Data setup and download shall be done by means of HGS.

HGS user's manual shall report the compatibility matrix of IDS qualified
P/Ns and HGS software version.

1 Data Log

Data resolution shall be as follows:

TOT   1 °C
N1    0.1% RPM
N2    1%  RPM
NR    1%  RPM
TRQ   1%
Time  1 second


1 Cumulative Log Set up

It shall be possible to modify the following data in the IDS NVM using the
HGS.

       48. Aircraft S/N
       49. Engine 1 and 2 S/N
       50. Main Transmission S/N
       51. Tail Transmission S/N
       52. Engine 1 and 2 EECU P/N
       53. DAU clock battery installation date.
       54. Date (current date). Data display format (DD-MM-YY or MM-DD-YY)
       55. Time (current time, Local/Zulu)
       56. Main Transmission TBO, TSN, TSO and TSO counter active/inactive.
       57. Tail Transmission TBO, TSN, TSO and TSO counter active/inactive.
       58. Engine 1 and Engine 2 TBO, TSN, TSO and TSO counter
           active/inactive.
       59. Engine 1 and Engine 2, Main and Tail Transmission Chip Detector
           activation
       60. Engine 1 and Engine 2 running times
       61. Engine 1 and Engine 2 OEI time counter
       62. Chip Burner activations
       63. Engine 1 and Engine 2 Power LIMITER ON activations
       64. Engine 1 and Engine 2 Training activations
       65. Total flight time
       66. Number of flights

The access to (and modification of) this data shall be possible only
through the HGS SETUP Mode when WOW = active.

2 Fault log

The faults shall be stored in the Fault Log

The following data shall be recorded for each type of fault:

|Type of Fault                                                              |
|Event              |Parameter(s) recorded                                  |
|First event        |Date/Time/Zone       |Duratio|Start of flight          |
|                   |                     |n      |(Date/Time)              |
|Second event       |Date/Time/Zone       |Duratio|Start of flight          |
|                   |                     |n      |(Date/Time)              |
|Third event        |Date/Time/Zone       |Duratio|Start of flight          |
|                   |                     |n      |(Date/Time)              |
|Most recent event  |Date/Time/Zone       |Duratio|Start of flight          |
|                   |                     |n      |(Date/Time)              |
|Number of          |Total Duration since |       |                         |
|occurrences        |last download        |       |                         |

The Fault Log is updated whenever a fault occurs.

All annunciations and maintenance messages shall be recorded as a type of
"fault" in the Fault Log as noted in Table 5.

The Fault Log shall start acquiring data as soon as practical after IDS
power is applied and be stored in NVM when any N1 goes >= to 12% then both
N1 < 12%.

The following data shall be recorded for each type of exceedance:

|            |                 |       |          |(Date/Time)   |            |

|            |                 |n      |          |flight        |status      |
|            |                 |       |          |(Date/Time)   |            |
|Third event |Date/Time/Zone   |Duratio|Peak value|Start of      | LIMITER ON |
|            |                 |n      |          |flight        |status      |
|            |                 |       |          |(Date/Time)   |            |
|Most recent |Date/Time/Zone   |Duratio|Peak value|Start of      |LIMITER ON  |
|event       |                 |n      |          |flight        |status      |
|            |                 |       |          |(Date/Time)   |            |
