|(since last download)        |       |          |              |            |
|Max Peak value               |       |          |              |            |
|(since last download)        |       |          |              |            |

The following exceedances shall be recorded:

TOT exceedance during start in Area A (engine 1 and 2)
TOT exceedance during start in Area B (engine 1 and 2)
TOT exceedance during start in Area C (engine 1 and 2)
TOT exceedance during start in Area D (Hung Start) (engine 1 and 2)
TOT exceedance while engine running (i.e. not during start) in Area A
(engine 1 and 2)
TOT exceedance while engine running (i.e. not during start) in Area B
(engine 1 and 2)
TOT exceedance while engine running (i.e. not during start) in Area C
(engine 1 and 2)
N1 exceedance in Area A (engine 1 and 2)
N1 exceedance in Area B (engine 1 and 2)
N1 exceedance in Area C (engine 1 and 2)
N2 exceedance in Area A (engine 1 and 2) except if DSCWD1 Bit 13 is active
Engine overtorque in Area A (engine 1 and 2)
Engine overtorque in Area B (engine 1 and 2)
Engine overtorque in Area C (engine 1 and 2)
Transmission overtorque exceedance in Area C (AEO)
Transmission overtorque exceedance in Area F (OEI)
83. NR high exceedance (upon activation of ROTOR HIGH Warning)
84. NR low exceedance (upon activation of ROTOR LOW Warning when DAU WOW
   discrete = inactive)
85. AIRSPEED (1)

The duration of the exceedance shall be defined as the total time in the
exceedance region.

The peak value of the exceedance shall be defined as the highest value
detected by the IDS during the exceedance.

If multiple exceedances occur, then each parameter shall be recorded
individually as a separate exceedance.

In case of two engine failures during the flight, the IDS shall continue to
record the NR parameter for autorotation monitoring.

The Exceedance Log shall start acquiring data when any PMS = IDLE or FLY
and any
N1 goes >= to 12% and stop acquiring and be stored in NVM when both N1 <
12% and
NR < 40%.

(1) Since for this exceedance the peak value shall not be recorded,  on  the
   Exceedance Log Screen and HGS display Blank Field.


4 Cumulative Log

The Cumulative Log shall record the following data:


        . Aircraft S/N
        . Date and Date format (when log is stored)
        . Time and Zone (when log is stored)
        . DAU clock battery install date
        . Aircraft Flight Time (the most recent flight and cumulative)
        . Start time of the most recent flight
        . Stop time of the most recent flight
        . Number of Flights (cumulative)
        . Main Transmission S/N
        . Main Transmission TBO, TSN, TSO and TSO active/inactive (TSN and
          TSO include flight time only)
        . Tail Transmission S/N
        . Tail Transmission TBO, TSN, TSO and TSO active/inactive (TSN and
          TSO include flight time only)
        . Number of Engine 1, Engine 2, Main Transmission, and Tail
          Transmission Chip Detector activations (the most recent flight and
          cumulative)
        . Number of Chip Burner activations (in the most recent flight and
          cumulative)
        . Engine 1 and engine 2 Start-up Time (the most recent flight)
        . Engine 1 and engine 2 Shutdown Time (the most recent flight)
        . Engine 1 and engine 2 Running time (cumulative)
        . Engine 1 and Engine 2 S/N
        . Engine 1 and Engine 2 TBO, TSN, TSO and TSO active/inactive (TSN
          and TSO include flight time only)
        . Engine 1 and Engine 2 OEI time counter
        . Engine 1 and Engine 2 EECU P/N
        . Number of Power LIMITER ON activations on Engine 1
        . Number of Power LIMITER ON activations on Engine 2
        . Number of Training activations on Engine #1
        . Number of Training activations on Engine #2
        . Engine 1 and Engine 2 Compressor Turbine cycle counts (Data Word
          26)
        . Engine 1 and Engine 2 Power Turbine cycle counts (Data Word 27)
        . Engine 1 and Engine 2 Impeller Turbine cycle counts (Data Word 28)
        . Engine 1 and Engine 2 Compressor Turbine Blade creep used life [%]


          (Data Word 29)
        . Engine 1 and Engine 2 Power Turbine Blade creep used life [%]
          (Data Word 30)

The Cumulative Log shall start acquiring data as soon as practical after
IDS power is applied and be stored in NVM when any N1 goes >= to 12% then
both N1 < 12%.


1 Data Counters setting and updating

At first installation on the aircraft, all the cumulative data counters
shall read zero (unless changed in the setup) and run up according to the
information provided by the pertinent partial counter.

However, when any dynamic system is changed, the associated cumulative
counters shall be reprogrammed through the HGS by manually inserting the
previous data taken from the log book and then the counters shall start
running up from that value.

2 Engine Start-up Time

Engine start-up time shall be defined as when N1 goes from <= 12% to >12%
(i.e. 12% threshold crossed upward).

3 Engine Shut-down Time

Engine shutdown time shall be defined as when N1 goes from >=50% to <50%
(i.e. 50% threshold crossed downward).

4 Engine 1 and Engine 2 Running Time

Engine running time shall increment if the following condition are met:

   a) If the NG (N1) signal is valid (not LOS), then the engine running time
      will increment if NG is greater than 50%.
   b) If the NG (N1) signal is not valid (LOS), then the engine running time
      will increment if NF (N2) is greater than 40% AND T6 is greater than
      345( C.

Notes:
        1) DAU does not have only T6 but T6-T1 on the raw lines, and T1 is
           available on RS422 (Word 8)
        2) If (N1 AND N2) OR (N1 AND T6) are not valid then the
           corresponding Engine Running Time will not increment.


5 Flight time (Engine, Transmissions and A/C Time)

Flight time shall be defined as the period of time between:

       a) The moment the weight-on-wheels (WOW) switch changes from "on-
          ground" status to "off-ground" status and stays there for longer
          than 5 seconds, and NR > 80%.


       b) The moment the WOW switch changes from "off-ground" status to "on-
          ground" status and stays there for longer than 5 seconds.

Each complete "off-ground/on-ground" cycle as described above shall be
counted and the duration stored as one flight.

Total flight time and number of aircraft flights shall be appended after
each flight.

At first installation on the aircraft, all counters shall read zero (unless
changed in the setup).

Engine, Transmission and A/C TSN and TSO Time counters shall increment
using flight time (flight time is as specified above).

6 Engine OEI Time Counter

When a TOT (MGT) exceedance in area A (2.5 min OEI) is detected and the  2.5
min time is not exceeded, then the Engine OEI Time Counter of  the  relevant
engine shall be incremented in the Cumulative Log.

  Note: One application of TOT 2.5 minutes rating for less then  20  seconds
      does not required the    incrementing  of the Engine OEI Time  Counter
      as per TOT limit chart. (see figure 5)

The counter shall be resettable through the HGS.

7 Activation/Deactivation of TSO Time Counters

The TSO time counters shall only increment while their respective TSO
active/inactive toggle is active. The TSO active/inactive toggles shall be
set using the HGS.


5 Time History Log Parameters

The IDS shall be capable of continuously recording N1, N2, TRQ, TOT of each
engine, #1 and #2 CLP and NR with 1 Hz sampling frequency.

The IDS shall be capable of storing the above mentioned parameters for at
least 5 hrs.

When the storing capacity has been achieved, data shall be overwritten with
a FIFO logic.

Recording shall start when any N1 raises above 12%.  The recording shall
stop when both N1 falls below 12%

Each record shall be Date/Time/Zone stamped

6 Power Assurance Check Log Parameters

On pilot request, through an EDU menu 1/3 push button, the IDS shall for 5
sec (at an acquisition rate of 1 Hz) store the following engine parameters:

    . Engine 1 and Engine 2 N2 (average)
    . Engine 1 and Engine 2 TRQ (average)
    . Engine 1 and Engine 2 TOT (average)
    . Engine 1 and Engine 2 N1 (average)

These Power Assurance Check parameter data shall be complemented by the
following information taken at the start of the acquisition:

    . Paltitude
    . OAT
    . IAS
    . Engine MAN (ON/OFF)
    . Flight IDLE (Display legend ON/OFF)
    . Date/time

The IDS shall be capable of keeping in memory 14 Power Assurance Checks.

Should the pilot request a Power Assurance Check when the 14 records are
full, the oldest one shall be overwritten (FIFO).


7 Status Log Parameters

The IDS shall be capable of continuously recording the status (active or
inactive) of the Faults listed in Table 5 and the Exceedances listed in
section 3.3.3.1.3 at 1 Hz sampling frequency.

The logic used to determinate the status of the Faults shall be per Table
5.

The logic used to determinate the status of the Exceedances shall be per
Figures 4 through 9 and section 3.3.5.2.1.

The IDS shall be capable of storing the above mentioned status for at least
1 hour.

Status recording shall start as soon as practical after IDS power is
applied.

Each record shall be Date/Time/Zone stamped.


2 Clearing/Updating of Log Data


1 Cumulative Log

The Cumulative Log information shall be manually modified only through the
HGS

2 Fault Log

The fault data shall be cleared only after a successful downloading into
the HGS

3 Exceedance Log

The exceedance data shall be cleared only after a successful downloading
into the HGS

4 Time History Log

The time history Log shall be cleared only after a successful downloading
into the HGS.

5 Power Assurance Check Log

The Power Assurance Check Log shall be cleared only after a successful
downloading into the HGS.

6 Status Log

The Status Log shall not be cleared after downloading into the HGS.

4 Display Formats


1 EDU 1

The EDU 1 shall present the following information

Two N1 indications
Two N2 indications
Two TOT indications
Two TRQ indications
One NR indication
Caution, warning and (limited) advisory annunciations

EDU 1 shall continuously display the information provided by both the EECUs
(which are the primary sources of information for all the essential engine
and rotor data) and automatically revert to the DAU in case of complete
EECU data bus failure (ECU DATA caution active).
For instance, should the EECU 1 data bus fail, then EDU 1 shall continue to
display the information directly provided by the EECU 2 and revert to the
DAU only for the information associated to the engine 1.

In the event that any single parameter is declared invalid by any EECU over
its data bus, then the EDU shall revert to the DAU only for that failed
parameter (if a back-up signal is available and valid) and continue to
display all the other data transmitted from the affected EECU.

For instance, if the TOT signal provided by the EECU 1 is declared invalid,
EDU 1 shall display the back-up TOT signal provided by the DAU, if
available and valid, and maintain the bus communication with the EECU 1 for
all the other valid parameters.

As far as the rotor speed signal is concerned, if it is declared invalid by
any EECU, EDU 1 shall use the information provided by the other EECU before
reverting to the DAU.

However, in order to increase the safety of flight and also ensure the
display moding function, in case of any EECU failure (ECU FAIL warning
active) or loss of any EECU data bus communication (ECU DATA caution
active), the IDS shall directly compute the Engine Out condition of the
affected engine and activate the relevant warning annunciation when N1 <
50%.

Control facility shall be provided to manually force either DAU Channel as
a primary source of information in lieu of the EECUs, if necessary.

The caution and warning annunciations shall be displayed in the lower part
of display area.

The other display options or menu functions can be selected by pressing the
M (Menu/Mode) key and it shall be possible to manually select the following
display formats or functions:

START
CRUISE
POWER CHECK
TEST
DAU Channels
DATA
MAINT
SWAP (Future Option)
MAINT TEST

However, the occurrence of a new caution and/or warning annunciation shall
always override the display of menu selection.

Figures 1and C9 shows the menu tree for EDU 1.
Table 9 describes the EDU 1 menu tree.
The display formats are shown in Annex C.

In the event of EDU 2 failure, EDU 1 shall display the Reversionary mode
and provide the same functionalities specified for EDU 2.

An IDS caution annunciation shall be displayed to inform the pilot about
the system failure condition.

2 EDU 2

The EDU 2 shall present the following information

Two engine oil pressure indications
Two engine oil temperature indications
One transmission oil pressure indication
One transmission oil temperature indication
Two fuel pressure indications
Two fuel quantity indications
Two fuel flow indications (if provided by the EECU)
Two primary hydraulic pressure indications
Two utility hydraulic pressure indications
Two DC ammeter indications
Two DC voltmeter indications
Two AC voltmeter indications
One Outside Air Temperature (OAT) indication
Advisory and status annunciations

EDU 2 shall continuously display the information provided by the DAU
Channel A (default mode) and automatically revert to the Channel B in the
event of a Channel A detected failure. In the unlikely event that both DAU
channels have detected failures, the EDU shall automatically revert back to
Channel  A (default mode).

Control facility shall be provided to manually force Channel B as a primary
source of information, if necessary.

In the event of EDU 1 failure, EDU 2 shall display the Reversionary mode
and provide the same functionalities specified for EDU 1.

An IDS caution annunciation shall be displayed to inform the pilot about
the system failure condition.

The advisory annunciations shall be displayed in the lower part of display
area and the advisories shall have precedence over the status
annunciations.

The other display options or menu functions can be selected by pressing the
M (Menu/Mode) key and it shall be possible to manually select the following
display formats or functions:

MAIN
AUX
POWER CHECK
TEST
DAU Channels
DATA
MAINT
COPY (Future Option)
MAINT TEST

However, the occurrence of a new advisory and/or status annunciation shall
not override the display of menu selection.

Figures 2 and C10 shows the menu tree for EDU 2.
Table 10 describes the EDU 2 menu tree.
The display formats are shown in Annex C.


3 Malfunction Indications

In case of loss of power supply to any IDS components, the IDS caution
annunciation may be displayed on EDU 1 or EDU 2 and recorded in the Fault
Log.


4 Ground Start-up Mode (EDU 1)

The ground start-up (START) mode shall be in accordance with Figure C1
and shall be displayed upon IDS initialization if EDUs and DAU are powered
up simultaneously, or when selected by the START key on EDU 1.

Linear scales of equal sensitivity featuring AEO range markings shall be
displayed with the associated pointers placed at the lower extreme of the
scales.
The color of each pointer and associated digital box shall match the color
of scale marking, as specified in 3.4.2.1.

In the event of any sensor failure to both the EECU and DAU inputs, the
affected digital box shall be displayed in white with 3 red dashes.

For the N2 parameters, a red FAIL legend shall be vertically displayed at
the bottom of the N2 pointer area of the affected engine. This legend shall
override the IDLE advisory annunciation, if active.

During the manual start-up procedure (i.e. EECU in Manual Mode), the
digital readouts of N1, TOT and TRQ parameters shall be surrounded by a
magenta box until the pertinent engine idle speed is attained (N1 > 50%) or
the ENG OUT warning signal becomes inactive.

The legend MAN (manual) shall be vertically displayed alongside the
pertinent TRQ pointer when the relevant signal is detected by the IDS.

This annunciation shall be displayed in yellow at the bottom of TRQ scale
of the affected engine, as shown in Figure C1.

The legend START shall be vertically displayed alongside the pertinent N1
pointer when the relevant signal is detected by the IDS.

This annunciation shall be displayed in green at the bottom of N1 scale of
the affected engine, as shown in Figure C1.
The legend IGN (ignition) shall be vertically displayed alongside the
pertinent TOT pointer when the relevant signal is detected by the IDS.

This annunciation shall be displayed in green at the bottom of TOT scale of
the affected engine, as shown in Figure C1.

The legend IDLE shall be vertically displayed alongside the pertinent N2
pointer when the relevant signal is detected by the IDS.

This annunciation shall be displayed in green at the bottom of N2 scale of
the affected engine, as shown in Figure C1.

The TOT start limits (red dots) shall be displayed as specified below.

             a) In AEO mode, display both the red dots when both engine N1
                speeds are ( 55%.

b) In OEI mode, display a single red dot only on the side of the failed
   engine when its N1 speed is ( 55%.

In order to reduce the pilot workload during the engine start-up sequence
(WOW = active), caution and warning annunciations shall be presented at
initial IDS power-up in priority order in accordance with the requirements
of Table 6.

As some annunciations may randomly be activated during pre-flight checks
(e.g MAIN UTIL CHRG, PLA, etc.), they shall be presented according to the
"last-in-first-out" requirement and remain as such even when the CLR switch
has been pressed. This will permit to remind to the pilot that some
abnormal annunciations are present in addition to the ones shown in Table
6.


5 Cruise Mode (EDU 1)

The purpose of this format is to present the primary powerplant parameters
such that their respective operating limits are set on the same horizontal
plane and the major part of the available screen area is allocated to the
flight operating range of parameter values.

The CRUISE mode shall be in accordance with Figure C2 and shall be
displayed upon IDS initialization if the conditions for the START format do
not exist (see para. 3.3.4.4 above), when selected by the CRUISE key on EDU
1, or as specified in Table 9.

The CRUISE format shall also be automatically presented on both EDUs in
case of complete loss of DAU (secondary data no longer available).

Unless otherwise specified, all scales shall be displayed with different
sensitivity and feature AEO range markings, with the associated pointers
placed at the lower extreme of the scales.

Unless otherwise specified, the scale sensitivity for N1, TOT, N2/NR
parameters should be in accordance with the following requirements:

Compressed scale from 0 to 20% of scale length (1 to 28 pixels)
Expanded scale from 20 to 100% of scale length
Red line (normalized) at 84% of scale length (118th pixel)

A radial white line shall be provided to indicate the change of scale
sensitivity and its value shall be as follows:

TOT   White line at 450 °C

The color of each pointer and associated digital box shall match the color
of scale marking, as specified in 3.4.2.1.

In the event of any sensor failure to both the EECU and DAU inputs, the
affected digital box shall be displayed in white with 3 red dashes.

For the N2 parameters, a red FAIL legend shall be vertically displayed at
the bottom of the N2 pointer area of the affected engine. This legend shall
override the IDLE advisory annunciation, if active.

During the manual start-up procedure (i.e. EECU in Manual Mode), the
digital readouts of N1, TOT and TRQ parameters shall be surrounded by a
magenta box until the pertinent engine idle speed is attained (N1 > 50%) or
the ENG OUT signal becomes inactive.

The legend MAN shall be vertically displayed alongside the pertinent TRQ
pointer when the relevant signal is detected by the IDS.

This annunciation shall be displayed in yellow at the bottom of TRQ scale
of the affected engine.

The legend START shall be vertically displayed alongside the pertinent N1
pointer when the relevant signal is detected by the IDS.

This annunciation shall be displayed in green at the bottom of N1 scale of
the affected engine.

The legend IGN shall be vertically displayed alongside the pertinent TOT
pointer when the relevant signal is detected by the IDS.

This annunciation shall be displayed in green at the bottom of TOT scale of
the affected engine.

The legend IDLE shall be vertically displayed alongside the pertinent N2
pointer when the relevant signal is detected by the IDS.

This annunciation shall be displayed in green at the bottom of N2 pointer
of the affected engine, as shown in Figure C1.

The two TOT start limits (red dots) shall be presented as specified in
3.3.4.4.


6 One Engine Inoperative (OEI) Mode (EDU 1)

If an engine fails during the flight (Engine Out signal active), there
shall be an OEI display format in accordance with Figure C3  which is a
subset of CRUISE mode.

If the ECU FAIL and/or the ECU DATA signal is active (that is the faulty
EECU is not capable to provide the ENG OUT signal to the IDS when the
associated engine fails), the IDS shall automatically determine the Engine
Out condition (back-up function) based upon the following requirement.

|Parameter   |A109S       |
|N1          |< 50%       |

When the above conditions occur (i.e. EECU failed and N1 signal lower than
the specified N1 limit), EDU1 shall display the relevant ENG OUT warning
annunciation and DAU shall record it in the Fault Log and also drive the
MWLs.

The above requirement shall apply all the time, regardless of the screen
format being used.

The OEI legend shall be vertically displayed alongside the pertinent N1,
TOT and TRQ pointers, in accordance with Figure C3, and color coded as
follows:

               . White indicates that the OEI format is being used.

               . Yellow indicates that the OEI power limits are authorized
                 by the EECU.

               . Red Blinking indicates that the time for the OEI power
                 limits is expired, as commanded by the EECU.

The TOT start limits (red dots) shall be suppressed until the PMS of
affected engine is set to Off, as specified in Annex B.

It shall be possible to present the OEI format both in CRUISE and
REVERSIONARY modes.
If the OEI condition is recovered (i.e. a successful relight of the failed
engine is accomplished), EDU 1 shall automatically revert to the CRUISE or
REVERSIONARY format (whichever applies) when the following requirements are
met:

WOW = inactive, and
Both PMS' = Fly, and
Both N2 signals matched within ( 0.2% error band for 3 second or greater.

The above requirements are specified in  order  to  take  into  account  the
engine acceleration characteristics up to Fly regime.

7 OEI Training Mode (EDU 1)

When the OEI Training mode is selected by the pilot and authorized by the
EECUs, there shall be a display format in accordance with Figure C4 which
is a subset of OEI mode.

The OEI Training mode shall be enabled when:

WOW = inactive, and
Both PMS' = Fly, and
Both EECUs = active and set to Training mode, and
No OEI or Autorotation condition exists

The N1, TOT and TRQ scales shall be rearranged in order to take into
account the new derated power limits, as specified in Annex A.

The OEI legend shall be displayed as specified in 3.3.4.6.

The TNG (training) legend shall be vertically displayed yellow alongside
the pertinent N1, TOT and TRQ pointers, as shown in Figure C4.

The TOT start limits and Torque transient limits shall be suppressed when
this screen format is being used.



EDU 1 shall automatically revert without time delay to the OEI mode when:

WOW = inactive, and
Both PMS = Fly (OEI Training discrete bit suppressed by the EECUs), and
No Autorotation condition exists

The transition from the OEI mode to the AEO mode shall occur based upon the
following requirement:

Both N2 signals matched within (0.2% error band for 3 seconds or greater


EDU1  shall  automatically  revert  without  time  delay  to  the   OEI   or
Autorotation  mode  in  case  of  single  or  dual  Engine  Out  conditions,
respectively.


8 Autorotation Mode (EDU 1)

When WOW = inactive, the N2/NR scale shall be rearranged to display the
Autorotation format with the NR limits defined in Annexes A or C when any
of the following conditions occurs:

Both engines fail (ENG OUT  signals active), or
Both engines running at idle speed (PMS' set to Idle position(, or
RPM split ((NR - N2-Eng. 1 ( 1%) and (NR - N2-Eng. 2 ( 1%)(, or
Both engine torque inputs ( 2% (TRQ 1 and TRQ 2 ( 2%).

Table 9 shows the display moding based on the Engine Out signal and the PMS
setting.

The screen format shall be in accordance with Figure C5.

In case of any EECU Failure, then the IDS shall automatically determine the
pertinent Engine Out condition based upon the requirement stated in
3.3.4.6.

It shall be possible to present the Autorotation format both in CRUISE and
REVERSIONARY modes.

When this format is being used, the IDS shall automatically set the trip
points for the generation of NR aural and visual warnings to the following
power-off limits:

143. Rotor High ( 110.5% RPM
Rotor Low <   94.5% RPM

The ROTOR LOW aural warning annunciation (AWG and horn) shall be suppressed
 when
NR < 80 ( 1% RPM.

9 Main Mode (EDU 2)

The main (MAIN) mode shall be in accordance with Figure C7 and shall be
displayed upon IDS initialization and/or when selected by the MAIN key on
EDU 2.

Unless otherwise specified in the Annexes, linear scales of equal
sensitivity shall be displayed, with the associated pointers placed at the
lower extreme of the scales.

The color of each pointer and associated digital box shall match the color
of scale marking, as specified in 3.4.2.1.

In the event of any sensor failure to the DAU input, the affected digital
box shall be displayed in white with 3 red dashes.

When any fuel pump failure occurs and the crossfeed valve is open, the fuel
quantity box associated to the other fuel tank shall appear in magenta,
meaning that this tank is feeding both engines (e.g. FUEL PUMP 1 = active
and XFEED = active, the No. 2 Fuel Quantity box shall appear in magenta).

However, when the Fuel Low signal is active, the color of associated fuel
box shall turn to yellow in order to enhance the failure condition
(priority requirement).

10 Auxiliary Mode (EDU 2)

The auxiliary (AUX) mode shall be in accordance with Figure C8 for
Retractable Landing Gear, with Figure C8A for Skid Landing Gear and shall
be displayed when selected by the AUX key on EDU 2.

Unless otherwise specified in the Annexes, linear scales of equal
sensitivity shall be displayed, with the associated pointers placed at the
lower extreme of the scales.

The color of each pointer and associated digital box shall match the color
of scale marking, as specified in 3.4.2.1.

In the event of any sensor failure to the DAU input, the affected digital
box shall be displayed in white with 3 red dashes.

When any of the following messages is active, the AUX page, if selected,
shall automatically revert to the MAIN page:


             |#1 OIL PRES      |XMSN OIL PRES    |#2 OIL PRES      |
|#1 OIL HOT       |XMSN OIL HOT     |#2 OIL HOT       |
|FUEL PUMP 1      |                 |FUEL PUMP 2      |



             11 Reversionary Mode (EDU 1 or EDU 2)

In the event of failure of one EDU, there shall be a REVERSIONARY display
mode available in the other EDU in accordance with Figure C6.

Any EDU shall enter the REVERSIONARY mode:

   a) manually, when commanded by pressing the ON/OFF switch to OFF on an
      EDU and is detected by the other EDU, or
   b) automatically, when a critical failure (e.g. EDU power supply failure)
      occurs at an EDU and is detected by the other EDU.

This format shall contain the following parameters:

(a)  Analog presentation


       1) N1
       2) TOT
       3) TRQ
       4) N2/NR

The N2 scale identifiers shall be removed from the screen when this mode is
being presented.

(b)  Digital presentation

        1) Engine oil pressure and temperature
        2) Transmission oil pressure and temperature
        3) Fuel pressure
        4) Primary hydraulic pressure
        5) Fuel quantity

As the digital boxes of secondary parameters are no longer available,
colored dots (yellow, or red) shall be provided on both sides of titles to
indicate the operational band in which the relevant parameter is working.

In the event of any sensor failure, the following shall occur:

   a) Primary parameter:           Pointer removed from the screen
   b) Secondary parameter:   Digital readout replaced by 3 red dashes

In the event of dual failure to the DAU, the CRUISE format shall be
automatically displayed on both EDUs, as the secondary parameters are no
longer available.

When this mode is being used, it shall be possible to present the OEI and
Autorotation formats, in a similar manner as described in 3.3.4.6 and
3.3.4.8.

12 Sensors Data (EDU 1 or EDU 2)

When the DATA key is pressed there shall be a display format in accordance
with Figure C12 .
All the legends shall be displayed in cyan, while all the variables shall
be displayed in white.

Pressing the CAL key when WOW = active, all the off-set errors produced by
the strain-gauge transducers shall be automatically and simultaneously
compensated by the DAU, such that all the pressure indications shown on EDU
2 read zero.

However, as the Utility Hydraulic Accumulators are normally pre-charged at
430 PSI approx., the calibration procedure shall have no effect on the
corresponding pressure indications, unless the measured off-set error is
below 3% F.S..

If any off-set error exceeds the value of ( 3% F.S., it shall be displayed
in yellow on the DATA page and the status message CHECK DATA shall be
displayed in the message area of EDU 2 when WOW = active to indicate that a
maintenance action is required.

The DAU CH-A CAL FAIL and/or DAU CH-B CAL FAIL caution annunciations shall
be displayed as shown in Figure C12 to alert the operator that any or both
the DAU channels have failed to accomplish the calibration procedure.

The above message(s) shall be suppressed once the RTN key is pressed.

The display of collective pitch position (CLTV PITCH POSN) information
transmitted by each EECU shall also be provided, as shown in Figure C12.
The range of their values is defined in  Table B1.


13 EDU Test Pattern

When the TEST key on EDU 1 or EDU 2 is pressed, the following test patterns
are displayed.

1 EDU 1


The EDU1 test pattern shall comprise the CRUISE format with the following
data displayed
(nominal values are shown)

|Parameter   |Value       |
|N1          |97.4%       |
|TOT         |820 °C      |
|TRQ         |100%        |
|NR/N2       |101%        |

The caution and warning annunciations that are stimulated by the DAU when
the test routine is in progress shall be presented in the message area
(i.e. FIRE DET, F LOW FAIL, etc.).

EDU 1 shall automatically return to its previously selected format after 10
seconds or when commanded through the activation of Menu key (whichever
applies).

2 EDU 2

The EDU 2 test pattern shall comprise the MAIN format with the following
data displayed.
(nominal values are shown)

|Parameter            |Value       |
|Engine Oil Pressure  |50 PSI      |
|Engine Oil           |100 °C      |
|Temperature          |            |
|XMSN Oil Pressure    |40 PSI      |
|XMSN Oil Temperature |100 °C      |
|Fuel Pressure        |20 PSI      |
|Hydraulic Oil        |1500 PSI    |
|Pressure             |            |
|Fuel Quantity        |As provided |
|                     |by the FCU  |
|OAT                  |+25 °C      |


EDU 2 shall automatically return to its previously selected format after 10
seconds or when commanded through the activation of Menu key (whichever
comes first).

In addition, for the advisory area of EDU 2 the following apply.


1 IDS Software Version or Status

The EDU 2 shall present in the left column of Advisory area a readout of
EEPROM data, consisting of the following legends:


         EDU 1 SW XXX  White, steady, to indicate the EDU S/W version number


         EDU 2 SW XXX  White, steady, to indicate the EDU S/W version number


         DAU-A SW XXX  White, steady, to indicate the DAU CH-A S/W version
                             number


         DAU-B SW XXX  White, steady, to indicate the DAU CH-B S/W version
                             number


If a failure exists, some or all of the above legends may be replaced by:

         DAU-A SW FAIL Red message flashing at 2 Hz to indicate that the DAU
                             CH-A BIT checksum failure is detected

         DAU-B SW FAIL Red message flashing at 2 Hz to indicate that the DAU
                             CH-B BIT checksum failure is detected

2 Display of IDS Built-in Test (BIT) Status

The EDU 2 shall present in the center column of Advisory area a readout of
current BIT status, consisting of the following legends:


         EDU 1 BIT PASS      Green message, steady, to indicate that the EDU
                             BIT test has passed


         EDU 2 BIT PASS      Green message, steady, to indicate that the EDU
                             BIT test has passed


         DAU-A BIT PASS      Green message, steady, to indicate that the DAU
                             CH-A BIT test has passed


         DAU-B BIT PASS      Green message, steady, to indicate that the DAU
                             CH-B BIT test has passed



If the BIT test is in progress or a failure exists, some or all of the
above legends may be replaced by:

         EDU 1 BIT FAIL      Red message flashing at 2 Hz to indicate that
                             at least one BIT routine has failed.


         EDU 2 BIT FAIL      Red message flashing at 2 Hz to indicate that
                             at least one BIT routine has failed.


         DAU-A BIT FAIL      Red message flashing at 2 Hz to indicate that
                             at least one BIT routine has failed.


         DAU-B BIT FAIL      Red message flashing at 2 Hz to indicate that
                             at least one BIT routine has failed.


The status display shall be continuously updated while the test patterns
are on the displays.
A FAIL status shall be displayed as soon as any failure is detected, even
though the IDS may not have completed its first pass.

14 Maintenance Test Mode

When the MAINT TEST is pressed, the EDU being tested shall activate all the
primary color pixels to permit the verification (in sequential order) of
their status condition (i.e. black/hot spots).
The screen test shall be accomplished as follows:

a) The red screen shall be presented when the MAINT TEST key is pressed.

b) Press the up scroll switch to activate the other color pixels, as
   follows:

         145. First press:  Green pixels
         146. Second press:       Blue pixels
         147. Third press:  White pixels
         148. Fourth press: Black pixels
         149. Fifth press:  Lighting test pattern
         150. Sixth press:  IDS STATUS screen (see para. 3.3.4.14.1)
         151. Seventh press:      Red pixels, and so on

c)    Press the "M" key to escape from the MAINT TEST mode (return to
initial Menu page).


1 IDS Status Page

When the IDS STATUS page is accessed from the MAINT TEST key (see para.
3.3.4.14), there shall be a display format in accordance with Figure C13.

All the titles shall be displayed in cyan, while the status conditions
shall be displayed according to the following requirement:

        152. PASS      Green
        153. INACTIVE  Green
        154. FAIL      Red
        155. Information not valid      3 Red dashes


       This page shall present the following status conditions:


       DAU-A BIT  DAU Channel A BIT status


       DAU-B BIT  DAU Channel B BIT status


       DAU-A L315 ARINC 429 Label 315 status from DAU-A to EDU


       DAU-B L315 ARINC 429 Label 315 status from DAU-B to EDU


       DAU-A L316 ARINC 429 Label 316 status from DAU-A to EDU


       DAU-B L316 ARINC 429 Label 316 status from DAU-B to EDU


       DAU-A CHKSUM    DAU-A checksum status


       DAU-B CHKSUM    DAU-B checksum status


       DAU-A COMM ARINC 429 communication status from DAU-A to EDU


       DAU-B COMM ARINC 429 communication status from DAU-B to EDU


       EDU BIT    On-side EDU BIT status


       ALT EDU BIT     Off-side (alternate) EDU BIT status


       EDU L320-A ARINC 429 Label 320 status from EDU to DAU-A


       EDU L320-B ARINC 429 Label 320 status from EDU to DAU-B


       EDU L321-A ARINC 429 Label 321 status from EDU to DAU-A


       EDU L321-B ARINC 429 Label 321 status from EDU to DAU-B


       #1 REPEAT-A     EECU 1 data status repeated by DAU-A



             #1 REPEAT-B    EECU 1 data status repeated by DAU-B


       #2 REPEAT-A     EECU 2 data status repeated by DAU-A


       #2 REPEAT-B     EECU 2 data status repeated by DAU-B


       ALT EDU IDS     The display of IDS caution is inactive on alternate
                       (off-side) EDU during normal operating conditions.
                       This status condition shall be displayed on EDU 1
                       only.


15 MAINTENANCE Mode

The selection of MAINT function shall permit the access to the following
maintenance logs (see Figure C11):

           a) Fault Log
           b) Exceedance Log
           c) Cumulative Log
           d) Power Assurance Check Log


1 Fault Log

The Fault Log can be accessed through the EDU.

The EDU Fault Log display format shall be in accordance with Figure C15.
Each type of fault shall be displayed in a last to occur first to display
order
.
The display of the Fault Log shall be possible only when the aircraft is on
ground
(WOW = active).

Once the Fault Log is accessed, all the titles/legends shall be displayed
in cyan, while all the variables except for the type of Fault message shall
be displayed in white.

The type of Fault messages shall be color coded as follows:

      Warnings   Red
      Cautions   Yellow
      Advisories Green

2 Exceedance Log

The Exceedance Log can be accessed through the EDU.

The EDU Exceedance Log display format shall be in accordance with Figure
C16, C16-1.

The display of Exceedance Log shall be possible only when the aircraft is
on ground
(WOW = active).

The Exceedances shall be displayed in last to occur first to display order.


Once the Exceedance Log is accessed, all the titles/legends shall be
displayed in cyan, while all the variables shall be displayed in white.

3 Cumulative Log

The Cumulative Log can be accessed through the EDU.

The EDU Cumulative Log display format shall be in accordance with Figure
C17, C17-1, C17-2, C17-3 and C17-4.

The display of the Cumulative Log shall be possible only when the aircraft
is on ground (WOW = active).

Once the Cumulative Log is accessed, all the titles/legends shall be
displayed in cyan, while all the variables shall be displayed in white.

The only way to modify the Cumulative Log shall be through the HGS. (using
the Setup mode)


4 Power Assurance Check Log

The Power Assurance Check Log can be accessed through the EDU.

The EDU Power Assurance Check Log display format shall be in accordance
with Figure C14.

Each Power Assurance Check shall be displayed in a last to occur first to
display order.

The display of Power Assurance Check Log shall be possible only when the
aircraft is on ground
(WOW = active).

Once the Power Assurance Check Log is accessed, all the titles/legends
shall be displayed in cyan, while all the variables shall be displayed in
white.


16 SETUP Mode

In SETUP Mode on the HGS, the following data shall be displayed.

   156. System password
   157. Date, date format, time and zone
   158. DAU clock battery installation date
   159. Aircraft S/N and Total Flight Time
   160. Number of aircraft flights
   161. Main Transmission S/N, TBO, TSN and TSO active/inactive
   162. Tail Transmission S/N, TBO, TSN and TSO active/inactive
   163. Engine 1 S/N, TBO, TSN and TSO active/inactive
   164. Engine 2 S/N, TBO, TSN and TSO active/inactive
   165. Engine 1 running time
   166. Engine 2 running time
   167. Engine 1 OEI time counter
   168. Engine 2 OEI time counter
   169. Engine 1 EECU P/N
   170. Engine 2 EECU P/N
   171. Engine 1 chip detector activation
   172. Engine 2 chip detector activation
   173. Main Transmission chip detector activation
   174. Tail Transmission chip detector activation
   175. Chip Burner activation
   176. Engine 1 LIMITER ON activation
   177. Engine 2 LIMITER ON activation
   178. Engine 1 training activation
   179. Engine 2 training activation


All the legends shall be displayed in cyan, while all the variables shall
be displayed in white.

Access to and manual modification of the foregoing data shall be possible
only through the HGS.

When the aircraft is on ground (WOW = active), it shall be possible to
manually accomplish the following through the HGS:

   180. Entry of system password (xxxxxxxxxx)
   181. Setting of date and format (DD-MM-YY or MM-DD-YY)
   182. Setting of time 24H LOCAL or ZULU identifier, (hh:mm)
   183. Setting of DAU clock battery installation date
   184. Entry of Aircraft S/N (xxxxx) and Total Flight Time (hhhhh:mm)
   185. Entry of number of aircraft flights (nnnnn)
   186. Entry of Main Transmission S/N (xxxxxxx) *, TBO (hhhh), TSN
        (hhhhh:mm),
        TSO (hhhh:mm) and active/inactive TSO timer.
   187. Entry of Tail Transmission S/N (xxxxxxx) *, TBO (hhhh), TSN
        (hhhhh:mm),
        TSO (hhhh:mm) and active/inactive TSO timer.
   188. Entry of Engine 1 S/N (xxxxxx), TBO (hhhh), TSN (hhhhh:mm), TSO
        (hhhh:mm) and active/inactive TSO timer.
   189. Entry of Engine 2 S/N (xxxxxx), TBO (hhhh), TSN (hhhhh:mm), TSO
        (hhhh:mm) and active/inactive TSO timer.
   190. Entry of Engine 1 running time (hhhhh:mm)
   191. Entry of Engine 2 running time (hhhhh:mm)
   192. Entry of Engine 1 OEI time counter (mm:ss)
   193. Entry of Engine 2 OEI time counter (mm:ss)
   194. Entry of Engine 1 EECU P/N (nnnnnnn-n-nnn) or (nnnnnn-n-nnn) *
   195. Entry of Engine 2 EECU P/N (nnnnnnn-n-nnn) or (nnnnnn-n-nnn) *
   196. Entry of Engine 1 chip detector activation (nnnnn)
   197. Entry of Engine 2 chip detector activation (nnnnn)
   198. Entry of Main Transmission chip detector activation (nnnnn)
   199. Entry of Tail Transmission chip detector activation (nnnnn)
   200. Entry of Chip Burner activation (nnnnn)
   201. Entry of Engine 1 LIMITER ON activation (nnnnn)
   202. Entry of Engine 2 LIMITER ON activation (nnnnn)
   203. Entry of Engine 1 training activation (nnnnn)
   204. Entry of Engine 2 training activation (nnnnn)


* For the parts which have the first portion of P/N or have the S/N
                  composed by 6 digit,
the P/N or S/N shall be Right Justify and blank filled on the left.inserted
                      as follow: blank space followed by 6 digit.



1 Entry of System Password

A system password shall be stored in the HGS.

The password shall be initialized to 0 when the HGS software is installed
on the computer.

Once the password has been entered, the change of all user-modifiable
maintenance data shall be denied, unless a correct password is entered
first, through on the HGS.

The password shall be a minimum of one character and a maximum of ten
characters.

2 Setting of Date

The date shall be stored in the DAU at supplier facility.

The date format stored in the DAU shall be set to MM-DD-YY at supplier
facility.

It shall be possible to set the date and change the format (DD-MM-YY or MM-
DD-YY) using the HGS.


3 Setting of Time

The LOCAL time shall be stored in the DAU at supplier facility.

It shall be possible to set the time and to select time zone designator as
either the LOCAL or ZULU time, using the HGS.


4 Setting of DAU Clock Battery Installation Date

The lithium batteries installation date shall be stored in the DAU at
supplier facility.

It shall be possible to set the date using the HGS.

5 Entry of Aircraft S/N and Total Flight Time

If the aircraft S/N is set to 00000 then the CHECK SETUP cyan annunciation
shall be displayed on EDU 2 when WOW = active, to prompt the operator that
the entry of aircraft S/N is necessary.

The aircraft S/N stored in the DAU shall be set to 00000 at the suppliers
facility.

The Total Flight Time stored in the DAU shall be set to 0:00 at the
suppliers facility.

It shall be possible to enter the aircraft S/N and Total Flight Time using
the HGS.

6 Entry of Number of Aircraft Flights

The number of aircraft flights stored in the DAU shall be set to zero at
the suppliers facility.

It shall be possible to enter the number of aircraft flights using the HGS.

7 Entry of Main Transmission S/N

The Main Transmission S/N stored in the DAU shall be set to 000000 at the
suppliers facility.

It shall be possible to enter the Main Transmission S/N using the HGS.

8 Entry of Main Transmission TBO, TSN and TSO

The Main Transmission TBO, TSN and TSO stored in the DAU shall be set to
zero at the suppliers facility.

The TSO active/inactive toggle stored in the DAU shall be set to inactive
at the suppliers facility.

It shall be possible to enter the Main Transmission TBO, TSN and TSO using
the HGS.

It shall be possible to change the TSO active/inactive toggle using the
HGS.

The TSN and TSO include flight time only.


9 Entry of Tail Transmission S/N

The Tail Transmission S/N stored in the DAU shall be set to 000000 at the
suppliers facility.

It shall be possible to enter the Tail Transmission S/N using the HGS.

10 Entry of Tail Transmission TBO, TSN and TSO

The Tail Transmission TBO, TSN and TSO stored in the DAU shall be set to
zero at the suppliers facility.

The TSO active/inactive toggle stored in the DAU shall be set to inactive
at the suppliers facility.

It shall be possible to enter the Tail Transmission TBO, TSN and TSO using
the HGS.

It shall be possible to change the TSO active/inactive toggle using the
HGS.

The TSN and TSO include flight time only.


11 Entry of Engine 1 and Engine 2 S/Ns :

The Engines S/N stored in the DAU shall be set to 000000 at the suppliers
facility.

It shall be possible to enter the Engines S/N using the HGS.


12 Entry of Engine 1 and Engine 2 TBOs, TSNs and TSOs

The Engines TBO, TSN and TSO stored in the DAU shall be set to zero at the
suppliers facility.

The TSO active/inactive toggle stored in the DAU shall be set to inactive
at the suppliers facility.

It shall be possible to enter the Engine TBO, TSN and TSO using the HGS.

It shall be possible to change the TSO active/inactive toggle using the
HGS.

The TSN and TSO include flight time only.


13 Entry of Engine 1 and Engine 2 Running Time

The Engines running time stored in the DAU shall be set to zero at the
suppliers facility.

It shall be possible to enter the Engines running time using the HGS.


14 Entry of Engine 1 and Engine 2 OEI Time Counter

The Engines OEI time counter stored in the DAU shall be set to zero at the
suppliers facility.

It shall be possible to enter the Engines OEI time counter using the HGS.


15 Entry of Engine 1 and Engine 2 EECU P/N

The Engine 1 and Engine 2 EECU P/N stored in the DAU shall be set to
0000000-0-000 at the suppliers facility.

It shall be possible to enter the Engine 1 and Engine 2 EECU P/N using the
HGS.


16 Entry of Engine 1 and Engine 2 Chip Detector Activations

The Engines number of chip detector activations stored in the DAU shall be
set to zero at the suppliers facility.

It shall be possible to enter the Engines number of chip detector
activations using the HGS.


17 Entry of Main Transmission Chip Detector Activations

The Main Transmission number of chip detector activations stored in the DAU
shall be set to zero at the suppliers facility.

It shall be possible to enter the Main Transmission number of chip detector
activations using the HGS.


18 Entry of Tail Transmission Chip Detector Activations

The Tail Transmission number of chip detector activations stored in the DAU
shall be set to zero at the suppliers facility.

It shall be possible to enter the Tail Transmission number of chip detector
activations using the HGS.


19 Entry of  Chip Burner Activations

The number of chip burner activations stored in the DAU shall be set to
zero at the suppliers facility.

It shall be possible to enter the number of chip burner activations using
the HGS.

20 Entry of Engine 1 and Engine 2 LIMITER ON Activations

The Engines number of LIMITER ON activations stored in the DAU shall be set
to zero at the suppliers facility.

It shall be possible to enter the Engines number of LIMITER ON activations
using the HGS.

21 Entry of Engine 1 and Engine 2 Training Activations

The Engines number of training activations stored in the DAU shall be set
to zero at the suppliers facility.

It shall be possible to enter the Engines number of training activations
using the HGS.

5 Annunciations


1 Warning, Caution, Advisory and Status/Maintenance Presentation

The EDU shall provide a dedicated area partitioned in 3 columns, each one
capable to display up to 3 lines (2 lines in reversionary mode) of 14
characters each, for warning, caution, advisory, and status/maintenance
annunciations.

The message area shall be placed in the lower portion of EDU screen.

Warning and Cautions shall activate dedicated attention getters (MWL and
MCL respectively) together with an acoustic getter (AWG) when applicable

The DAU shall provide an interface for a remotely Master Reset Push-button
to be used to reset all the attention getters (MWLs and MCLs) and the AWG
when they are active.
The remote reset signal shall be a ground/open type (GND = active).

The presentation of warning, caution, advisory and status/maintenance
annunciations shall be as follows:


1 Warnings

Warnings shall be color coded Red.

Warnings shall have precedence over cautions, advisories, and status
messages.

The occurrence of a warning annunciation shall always override, if
necessary, the display of menu selection.

When a warning signal is displayed, the IDS shall make the two MWLs to
blink at a flash rate of 4±1 Hz. The IDS shall disable the MWLs when a
Remote MWL Reset discrete is active.

Should a failure occur to the blinking circuit, then a "steady-on"
condition shall be provided for the MWLs, once activated.

Warning messages shall remain presented until the generating condition no
longer exists.

With each new warning, the DAU shall activate the AWG master aural warning
signal, except for the following failure conditions, which shall activate
dedicated associated aural signals:

   4. ENG FIRE
   5. ENG OUT
   6. ROTOR HIGH
   7. ROTOR LOW

The appropriate AWG signal shall remain active until the warning condition
no longer exists or any warning reset signal is activated.

All warning condition occurrences noted in Table 5  shall be recorded in
the Fault Log along with their start time and date

The following warning annunciation shall be foreseen:

|TABLE 1 - Partitioning of Warning Annunciations                      |
|(Not in priority order)                                              |
|Left Column           |Center Column         |Right Column          |
|ENG 1 FIRE            |ROTOR HIGH            |ENG 2 FIRE            |
|ENG 1 OUT             |ROTOR LOW             |ENG 2 OUT             |
|#1 ECU FAIL           |ROTOR BRK ON          |#2 ECU FAIL           |
|#1 OIL PRES (1)       |XMSN OIL PRES         |#2 OIL PRES (1)       |
|(5 sec. Delay)        |                      |(5 sec. delay)        |
|                      |XMSN OIL HOT          |                      |
|                      |BATT HOT              |                      |
|                      |BATT DISCH (1)        |                      |
|                      |ELECTRICAL (1,2)      |                      |
|                      |BAGGAGE SMOKE         |                      |
|                      |NOSE WHL UNLK         |                      |


Note:
(1)   This message shall be provided for the CAA variant.
(2)   This message shall be displayed when both the DC GEN cautions are
    active, even if they are suppressed in flight according to Table 7.

2 Cautions

Cautions shall be color coded Yellow.

Cautions shall have precedence over advisories, and status messages.

When a caution signal is displayed, the IDS shall make the two MCLs to
blink at a flash rate of 4±1 Hz. The DAU shall disable the MCLs when a
Remote MCL Reset discrete is active.

The occurrence of a caution annunciation shall always override, if
necessary, the display of menu selection.

Should a failure occur to the blinking circuit, then a "steady-on"
condition shall be provided for the MCLs, once activated.

All caution condition occurrences noted in Table 5 shall be recorded in the
Fault Log along with their start time and duration.

Caution messages shall remain presented until the generating condition no
longer exists.

The following caution annunciation shall be foreseen:

|TABLE 2- Partitioning of Caution Annunciations                         |
|(Not in priority order)                                                |
|Left Column               |Center Column      |Right Column            |
|#1 OIL PRES (1) (5 sec.   |XMSN OVTRQ         |#2 OIL PRES (1) (5 sec. |
|delay)                    |                   |delay)                  |
|#1 OIL HOT                |TGB OIL CHIP       |#2 OIL HOT              |
|#1 OIL CHIP               |XMSN OIL CHIP      |#2 OIL CHIP             |
|#1 DCU                    |MGT MATCH          |#2 DCU                  |
|#1 ECU MAINT              |RPM SELECT         |#2 ECU MAINT            |
|#1 PMS                    |ATTD OFF           |#2 PMS                  |
|#1 PLA                    |FLOATS ARMED       |#2 PLA                  |
|#1 FIRE DET               |HOOK ARMED         |#2 FIRE DET             |
|#1 HOT START              |HOOK OPEN          |#2 HOT START            |
|#1 FUEL LOW               |LANDING GEAR (4)   |#2 FUEL LOW             |
|#1 F LOW FAIL             |AWG FAIL           |#2 F LOW FAIL           |
|#1 FUEL FLTR              |CABIN DOOR         |#2 FUEL FLTR            |
|#1 A/F F FLTR             |IDS                |#2 A/F F FLTR           |
|FUEL PUMP 1               |DAU MISCMP-P       |FUEL PUMP 2             |
|FUEL DRAIN 1              |DAU MISCMP-S       |FUEL DRAIN 2            |
|                          |(future option)    |                        |
|#1 DC GEN                 |BUS TIE            |#2 DC GEN               |


|TABLE 2- Partitioning of Caution Annunciations  (cont'd)                 |
|(Not in priority order)                                                  |
|Left Column                 |Center Column      |Right Column            |
|#1 GEN CTL                  |BATT OFF (2)       |#2 GEN CTL              |
|INV 1                       |EXT PWR ON (3)     |INV 2                   |
|SERVO 1                     |ROTOR BRK          |SERVO 2                 |
|MAIN UTIL PRES (4) (8 sec.  |ROTOR BRK PRES (3) |EMER UTIL PRES (4)      |
|Delay)                      |                   |                        |
|MAIN UTIL CHRG (3) (4)      |PARK BRK ON (3)    |EMER UTIL CHRG (3) (4)  |
|(3 sec. delay)              |                   |                        |
|SAS 1 OFF                   |UTIL DOOR (3)      |SAS 2 OFF               |
|VG 1                        |HOIST CUT ARMD     |VG 2                    |
|NOSE DOOR                   |HOIST CABL LKD     |BAG DOOR                |
|#1 OVSPD                    |BATT DOOR          |#2 OVSPD                |
|#1 OVSPD DET                |TRQ LIMITER        |#2 OVSPD DET            |
|#1 OVSPD TEST               |EFIS FAN (6 sec.   |#2 OVSPD TEST           |
|                            |delay)             |                        |
|#1 ECU DATA                 |MAIN RTR SERVO     |#2 ECU DATA             |
|#1 EAPS PRES (6 sec. delay) |                   |#2 EAPS PRES (6 sec.    |
|                            |                   |delay)                  |
|#1 TOT LIMITER              |                   |#2 TOT LIMITER          |
|#1 PLA MOTOR (2 sec. delay) |                   |#2 PLA MOTOR (2 sec.    |
|PITOT 1 FAIL                |                   |delay)                  |
|                            |                   |PITOT 2 FAIL            |
|FIRE BTL 1                  |                   |FIRE BTL 2              |

Notes:
(1)   This message shall be provided for the RAI variant.
(2)   This message shall be provided for the CAA variant.
(3)   This message shall not activate the MCLs, nor be stored in NVM.
(4)   This message shall be provided only for Retractable Landing Gear
Option.

The following dedicated yellow legends shall also be foreseen and displayed
as follows:

MAN   (EECU in Manual Mode)  at  the  bottom  of  corresponding  TRQ  analog
       pointer (vertical displacement).

OEI   alongside the corresponding N1, TOT and TRQ  analog pointers and just
       below the red line.   This indication shall be displayed in yellow
       when the 2.5 minute OEI limit is commanded by the EECU and displayed
       in red when the OEI time is elapsed (see Annex B, Table B4 for more
       details).

TNG   (OEI Training Mode) at the bottom of corresponding  N1,  TOT  and  TRQ
       analog pointers (vertical displacement).



       3 Advisories Messages

Advisories shall be color coded Green.

Advisories shall have precedence over status annunciations and shall remain
presented until the generating condition no longer exists.

The advisory signals shall not activate any external attention getter and
shall never override the display of menu selection (if any).

Except for IDLE, Advisories shall not be recorded in the Fault Log


|TABLE 3 - Partitioning of Advisory Annunciations                     |
|(Not in priority order)                                              |
|Left Column           |Center Column         |Right Column          |
|PITOT 1 HEAT          |FD DECPLED            |PITOT 2 HEAT          |
|#1 EAPS ON            |LANDING LT ON         |#2 EAPS ON            |
|ECS ON                |AUTOTRIM OFF          |VENT ON               |
|CLTV BRK OFF          |FT OFF                |LIMITER ON            |
|                      |HOIST ON              |OXYGEN CLOSED         |
|                      |                      |                      |
|                      |                      |                      |

The following dedicated advisory legends shall be displayed as follows:

START (Starter  On)  at  the  bottom  of  corresponding  N1  analog  pointer
       (vertical displacement).

IGN   (Ignition On) at the bottom of corresponding TOT analog pointer
       (vertical displacement).

XFEED (Crossfeed Valve On) between the two fuel quantity digital readouts
       in the MAIN and AUX  modes and in the center column of message area
       in the EDU Reversionary mode, (displayed as FUEL XFEED).

IDLE  at the bottom of corresponding N2 analog pointer (vertical
       displacement).
      This legend shall be overridden by the red FAIL legend, if active.



       4 Status/Maintenance Messages

Status/Maintenance messages shall be color coded as Cyan.

The Status/Maintenance signals shall not activate any external attention
getter.

The Status/Maintenance signals shall never override the display of menu
selection (if any).

When an Exceedance Log event has been detected or stored in the IDS (since
the Exceedance Log was last downloaded), the EXCEEDANCE annunciation shall
be displayed on EDU 2 when WOW = active.

Once the Exceedance Log has been successfully downloaded by the HGS, the
EXCEEDANCE annunciation shall be cleared (if displayed).

When a Fault Log event (except IDLE advisory) has been detected or stored
in the IDS (since the Fault Log was last downloaded), the MAINTENANCE
annunciation shall be displayed on EDU 2 when WOW = active.

Once the Fault Log has been successfully downloaded by the HGS, the
MAINTENANCE annunciation shall be cleared (if displayed).

If the Usage Log BIT detects a failure, then the secondary EDU shall
display MAINT LOG A or
MAINT LOG B (depending on which channel failed) in the right column of EDU
2 in cyan when
WOW = Active, to alert the maintenance personnel that a problem has been
detected, and let them to know which DAU channel has suspect Usage Log
data.

The cyan MAINTENANCE annunciation shall have a lower priority with respect
to the EXCEEDANCE annunciation.

8 Exceedances

This is the logic to apply for the exceedance time counters.


Over MGT Limit-All Conditions (Except Starting) Figure 5 and NG Overspeed
      Limit Figure 7


         . If we are in Take-off area, the counter counts for 300 sec
           before confirming that the allowed period is expired and enter
           in AREA B. Once in AREA B the counter continues to count.


         . If we are in Take-off area, and enter in AREA A (2.5 Minute)
           there are two scenarios:


        Scenario 1:
        The first is that the excursion into 2.5 minute area  is  confirmed
        (confirmed means that the 20 sec allowed period  is  elapsed).  The
        take-off counter stops counting and 2.5 minutes counter starts.


        It may happen that an  excursion  into  the  2.5  minutes  band  is
        confirmed, used for less than 2.5 minutes,  then  the  2.5  minutes
        band is left, and we go back into the take-off band. In this  case,
        as soon as the 2.5 minutes band is left, the  2.5  minutes  counter
        stops (and if MGT time is consumed, the cumulative counter  updated
        when required), and the take-off counter shall resume counting from
        the value reached at the time it stopped before cross  of  the  2.5
        minutes limit occurred.


        Scenario 2:
        The second is that the excursion  into  2.5  minutes  area  is  not
        confirmed (i.e., 2.5 minutes power used less than 20 seconds)     ,
        the 2.5 minutes  counters  does  not  increment  and  the  take-off
        counter continues to count the time spent in  2.5  minutes  rating.
        (i.e. if we leave the take-off band going into 2.5 minutes band for
        5 sec., those 5 sec. will be counted by the take-off counter).


        The above scenarios, should cover all the possible situation.


        In addition, we can state that generally  the  applicable  approach
        is:


         . If you enter into a band from the top,  the  allowance  time  is
           still calculated from the  moment  you  entered  the  band  from
           below.
           (i.e. TOT above 840° C in not start condition)
         . If you enter into a band from the bottom, allowance  times  must
           be taken into account.
         . If you drop power below the take-off band, all the single
           excursion OEI counters are reset to 0 (all allowance times are
           restored, i.e. 20 sec. for 2.5 minutes band and 5 minutes for
           take-off band).
         . If an excursion into any band is confirmed, the allowance time
           shall be taken into account  in the total exceedance time
           computation.



a) Hot Start Limits


                    #1 (#2) HOT START - A:    First event (n) in Area A
                    #1 (#2) HOT START - B:    First event (n) in Area B
                    #1 (#2) HOT START - C:    First event (n) in Area C
                    #1 (#2) HUNG START: First event (n) in Area D
                  Refer to Figure 4 of this report for hot start limits.
To prevent multiple Exceedances a hysteresis band with this value +5(C/-0(C
shall be applied.

b) Engine Overtemperature Limits


                  #1 (#2) TOT - A: 2.5 minutes limit in Area A
                  #1 (#2) TOT - B: First event (n) in Area B (AEO only)
                  #1 (#2) TOT - C: First event (n) in Area C
        Refer to Figure 5 of this report for overtemperature limits.
 To prevent multiple Exceedances a hysteresis band with this value +5(C/-0(C
                              shall be applied.


c) Engine Overtorque Limits


                  #1 (#2) TORQUE - A:   2.5 minutes limit in Area A
                  #1 (#2) TORQUE - B:   First event (n) in Area B
                  #1 (#2) TORQUE - C:   No limit in Area C (continuous OEI)
                  Refer to Figure 6 of this report for engine overtorque
                  limits.
 To prevent multiple Exceedances a hysteresis band with this value +0.5%/-0%
                              shall be applied.


d) N1 Overspeed Limits


                  #1 (#2) N1 - A:  2.5 minutes limit in Area A
                  #1 (#2) N1 - B:  First event (n) in Area B (AEO only)
                  #1 (#2) N1 - C:  First event (n) in Area C
                  Refer to Figure 7 of this report for N1 overspeed limits.
To prevent multiple Exceedances a hysteresis band with this value +1.5%/-0%
shall be applied.


e) N2 Overspeed Limits


                  #1 (#2) N2:      First event (n) in Area A
                  Refer to Figure 8 of this report for N2 overspeed limits.
To prevent multiple Exceedances a hysteresis band with this value +0.5%/-0%
                 shall be applied.


f) Transmission Overtorque Limits


                XMSN TRQ - C:      First event (n) in Area C
                XMSN TRQ - F:      First event (n) in Area F
                Refer to Figure 9 of this report for the transmission
                  overtorque limits.
To prevent multiple Exceedances a hysteresis band with this value +0.5%/-0%
                 shall be applied.

g) Rotor Limits


                ROTOR HI:    First event
                ROTOR LOW:   First event
                Refer to section 3.3.5.2.1 for limits.

9 Prioritization

Messages shall be grouped by urgency level, as follows:

a) EDU 1

Warnings shall be presented on top of the annunciation area of the display,
followed by cautions.

The "last-in" message shall always go on the top of the dedicated screen
area and shall displace the existing list, if any, by the necessary number
of lines downward.

In order to avoid the "cascade" effect when a certain failure condition
occurs, the event related annunciations shall be suppressed in accordance
with the requirements of Table 7.

b) EDU 2

Advisories shall be presented on top of the annunciation area of the
display, followed by status/maintenance annunciations.

The "last-in" message shall always go on the top of the dedicated screen
area and shall displace the existing list, if any, by the necessary number
of lines downward.

10 Minimum Message Duration

It is possible for a failure condition to exist for such a short period of
time that the pilot may not have a chance to identify the condition, even
though the condition has been cured or otherwise ended.

Therefore, a minimum message display time of 5 seconds shall be provided
for the following caution signals:

           205. #1 OIL CHIP
           206. #2 OIL CHIP
           207. XMSN OIL CHIP
           208. TGB OIL CHIP
           209. #1 FUEL LOW
           210. #2 FUEL LOW

The IDS shall immediately remove the annunciation if the corresponding
signal is no longer active after 5 seconds.

The transient messages if required by Table 5 to be recorded in the Fault
Log, shall always be logged in the Fault Log.


11 Message Display Overflow

When the number of active messages exceeds the capacity of the annunciation
area, the additional messages shall be stored in the overflow memory and
the scroll switch shall be used to display them.

The annunciation area shall provide an indication of "more" pages above or
below the one being displayed.

If a new alert condition arises, the new message shall be placed on top of
the list of messages belonging to the same criticality level (i.e. new
warning message on top of warning list and new caution message on top of
caution list).

2 DAU Generated Annunciation

Most of the annunciation are generated in response to activation of input
discrete signals. The following annunciations are, are however generated
internally by the DAU in response to sensed conditions as specified below.

1 Rotor Speed Warning Indication

During normal operation, the DAU shall process the rotor speed (NR) signal
provided by any EECU and provide a warning indication when:

|Rotor RPM      |Power-on       |Power-off      |
|High           |NR (105.5%     |NR (110.5%     |
|Low            |NR <95.5%      |NR <94.5%      |

If the NR signal provided by any EECU is declared invalid, the DAU shall
use the one provided by the other EECU (if available and valid); otherwise,
it shall use the signal provided by a separate NR pick-up coil.

For each of the above conditions (Rotor High, Rotor Low) the DAU shall
provide an independent ground discrete to the AWG for the appropriate aural
warning presentation to enhance the pilot response time, and shall activate
the MWLs. An independent ground output to the Rotor Low Horn shall also be
provided.

The aural warning indication shall remain active until the warning
condition no longer exists, any warning reset signal is activated, or NR <
80 ( 1 %.


The limit exceedance (peak  value and duration) shall be recorded in the
Exceedance Log, while the Fault  condition shall be recorded in the Fault
Log.


2 Transmission Overtorque Indication


During normal operation, the DAU shall compute the total transmission
rating using the engine torque signals provided by the EECUs and display a
caution annunciation when the power limit is above the following nominal
values:

|Condition      |A109S            |
|AEO            |TRQ 1 + TRQ 2    |
|               |(215% (968 SHP)  |
|OEI            |TRQ 1 or TRQ 2   |
|               |(162.5% (730 732 |
|               |SHP)             |

If the torque signal provided by any EECU is declared invalid, the DAU
shall use its equivalent back-up parameter, if available and valid.
When the above condition is met, the DAU shall also output a discrete
ground signal to the AWG to enable the generation of corresponding aural
tone and activate the MCLs.

The EDU 1 shall also start a timer function and after 4 seconds the above
caution annunciation shall be displayed flashing, until the transmission
rating returns below the specified limit.

The ground output signal shall remain active until the caution condition  no
longer exists or any  caution reset signal is activated.


The limit exceedance (peak value and duration)  shall  be  recorded  in  the
Exceedance Log,


3 Engine Air Particle Separator (EAPS) Interface


The DAU shall provide the following interfaces for the optional engine 1
and engine 2 air particle separator (EAPS):

211. One 28VDC/open input per engine, 28 VDC = active, associated to the
  ON/OFF power switch.
212. One ground/open input per engine, ground = active, associated to the
  air pressure switch. Only the High side of pressure switch will be
  connected to ground, while the Low side will always remain open.

EDU 2 shall display the green EAPS ON advisory message (one per engine)
when the power switch is activated and EDU 1 shall display the yellow EAPS
PRES caution message (one per engine) according to the following
requirements:

|Power Switch    |Pressure Switch |EAPS ON         |EAPS PRES       |
|OFF             |High (closed)   |Inactive        |Active          |
|OFF             |High (open)     |Inactive        |Inactive        |
|ON              |High (closed)   |Active          |Inactive        |
|ON              |High (open)     |Active          |Active          |




The EAPS PRES caution shall be displayed after a 6 sec. delay from input
             activation.



             4 HOT START

When an Exceedance Log Hot Start in area A, B or C event (see Figure 4) has
been detected or stored in the IDS (since the Exceedance Log was last
downloaded) the #1 HOT START or #2 HOT START (as appropriate) caution
annunciation shall be displayed on EDU 1.

Once the Exceedance Log has been successfully downloaded by the HGS the #1
HOT START and #2 HOT START caution annunciation shall be cleared (if
displayed).


5
VNE Calculation in KIAS

The DAU shall provide an output signal (Ground = Active) on Cavity B Pin
A12 to drive the AWG aural message (Airspeed) when IAS > IAS limit.

The status active and duration of AIRSPEED shall be logged in the
EXCEEDANCE LOG and in the STATUS LOG

Note:
IAS = aircraft IAS
IAS_Limit = aircraft Vne expressed in IAS
PA = Pressure Altitude
OAT = Outside Ambient Temperature
Airspeed Limitations Vne - KIAS = IAS_Limit
Hp = PA

    a. IAS_Limit = function of (PA, OAT);
   b. IAS_Limit law with PA, OAT: in accordance with the following  table  a
      linear interpolation is requested for OAT and PA (Hp) not reported  in
      the table below

Note: For temperature < - 40 ° C or  > 50 ° C, use the values reported in
the columns
           - 40 ° C and 50 ° C
     For altitude >20000 feet use the values reported in the columns 20000
feet

The character cell shall be total character as stored and displayed and
shall include both the character font and the spacing before and after the
font.

The space between characters shall be 3 pixels (2 pixels before and 1 pixel
after each character font).

Note:  All references to "pixel" shall be a color pixel which is made up of
3 RGB sub-pixels (0.119 x 0.357 mm). A pixel is 0.351 mm wide by 0.357 mm
high.

6 Width to Height Ratio

The requirements of 3.4.2.2.3 shall apply.

7 Stroke Width

One very significant factor in alphanumeric symbol legibility is the
boldness of the strokes that define the symbol. In general, the stroke
width shall be one local line width.

8 Vertical Spacing

Vertical spacing, the space between adjacent characters on the same line,
is not a critical design parameter with respect to visibility requirements.
The recommendations of 50% of the character width shall be to adopted for
all character sets.

9 Horizontal Spacing

Horizontal spacing, the space between adjacent lines of characters, can
have a significant impact on the legibility of full pages of alphanumeric
characters.

MIL-STD-1472D specifies that characters be separated by a half-character
space; therefore, the requirements of 3.4.2.2.3. shall apply.


3 Range Marking Requirements

The color marking scheme shall generally consist of three colors:

  213. Red,
  214. Green,
  215. Yellow.

The color red shall indicate an operating condition beyond authorized
limits and requires a specific pilot action.

Where appropriate, the maximum should be the limit for takeoff operation.

On a vertical or horizontal scale instrument, a red line or stripe applied
across the scale to mark the maximum and minimum authorized operating
limits is comparable to the red radial line applied to round dial
instruments.

Red lines should be located so that the edge nearest the normal operating
range is placed on the limit value.

To minimize confusion, the use of the multiple red lines should be avoided,
except for certain "conditional limits".


Multiple red lines can be displayed one at a time with the switching
accomplished automatically.

If possible, multiple red lines should have distinguishing features, such
as one solid line and one dashed line, or lines of different lengths.

   a) The color green shall indicate a normal condition for operation, both
      ground and flight.

Where applicable, the high value end of the green band shall indicate the
maximum limit for normal operation and the low value end of the green band
shall indicate the minimum limit for normal operation.

   b) The color yellow shall indicate either a takeoff or cautionary range
      where limited operation is permissible.

   c) Green, red, yellow lines or stripes parallel to the scale with the
      ends of the lines at the specified range limit graduations are
      practical methods of conveying the same type of information as
      provided by the colored arcs on round dial instruments.

If necessary, yellow and red markings can be subdued until the parameter
being monitored reaches the caution or warning value.

At that time the respective color shall be highlighted as specified in
para. 3.4.2.1


4 Special Symbols

Engines and main transmission are restricted to given ranges of operation
for safety.

These ranges are monitored by observing certain measurements, some of which
need to be observed only during dynamic conditions of operation and are of
concern for short period of time.

These short term restrictions are called "transient limits" and are
distinct from the more familiar long term "steady state limits".

The transient limits shall be marked as follows:

A red triangle to indicate an inflight transient condition above the normal
maximum red line
A red dot to indicate a transient condition during engine start-up
operation

Red lines should always be the dominant marking.

Some transient limit markings may be removed from the display when they are
not applicable to the operating condition (display declutter).

5 Range Markings

Annex A shows the range markings applicable to the A109S helicopter,
equipped with Pratt & Whitney Canada (P&WC) PW207C engine.

                    Height:  11 pixels

                    Width:   30 pixels

                    Width:   27 pixels
                    Height:  17 pixels
