4 Reliability and Maintainability Requirements



1 Basic Reliability Requirements


Basic reliability covers all failures attributable to the equipment (i.e.
not only safety critical failures) which demand maintenance manpower at the
aircraft level. The basic reliability requirement is expressed as MTBF
(Mean Time Between Failure).

The MTBF for any equipment delivered by the Supplier shall not be less than
:
           218. EDU:   > 2300 hours
           219. DAU:   > 2500 hours


The conversion factor shall be assumed to be 1.25, i.e. 1 flying hour =
1.25 equipment operating hours.


1 Tasks to be undertaken by the Supplier


Compliance with the above reliability requirement shall be demonstrated by
analysis assuming an ambient temperature of 50(C. The analysis shall be
performed according to MIL-HDBK-217F.

Documents to be delivered are as follows:

     1. Reliability Prediction.

     2. Failure Analysis and Corrective Action Reports, as required by the
        Customer.

The Reliability Prediction shall be delivered within three months of
receipt of this specification. In the event of design changes following the
receipt of the equipment order, the Supplier shall submit revised
documentation within two months of the change being agreed.

The predicted MTBF will be verified using data obtained during flight
trials and/or in-service data sources. If the above MTBF requirements are
not achieved, the Supplier shall introduce modifications free of charge in
order that the equipment will meet the specified reliability requirements.



2 Maintainability Requirements



1 Qualitative Requirements



The following qualitative maintainability requirements shall apply as
applicable.

     i. The equipment shall not require any preventive maintenance to
        maintain the basic MTBF specified. The equipment shall be maintained
        on an 'on condition' basis with restoration only required in the
        event of failure.


    ii. Interchangeability of components is required.

   iii. The need for Aircraft Ground Equipment (AGE) shall be minimised. The
        use of special tools shall only be required where
        standard/commercial tools are not available.

    iv. Connectors in close proximity shall be designed to prevent the
        possibility of incorrect or crossed-connections.

     v. On A/C access to test/check connectors shall be possible without
        removing the other equipment parts

    vi. No adjustment should be required for the equipment before and after
        its installation on A/C.

   vii. The system Line Replaceable Units shall be repairable and repairs
        should be possible at a Supplier approved facility.



      2 Quantitative Requirements


1 Preventive Maintenance

The equipment shall not require any preventive maintenance or overhaul to
maintain the basic MTBF specified. The equipment shall be maintained on an
'on condition' basis with restoration only required in the event of
failure.

2 Corrective Maintenance

The on-aircraft replacement/recovery time of each equipment LRU shall be
less than 30 minutes, assuming ready access.

The above times include diagnosis, repair and test.

3 Tasks to be undertaken by the Supplier

During the design and development phase, compliance with the above
maintainability requirements shall be demonstrated by analysis.

A full maintainability analysis shall be delivered as a means of compliance
for each of the above requirements. This analysis shall be delivered within
three months of receipt of the equipment order. In the event of design
changes following the receipt of the equipment order, the Supplier shall
submit revised documentation within two months of the change being agreed.


3 Qualitative Testability Requirements


1 Failure Detection Capability

All hardware failure which in combination with an other hardware failure or
software fault can result in a functional failure classified as Hazardous
(refer to the Functional Failure Critically Table) shall be detected by the
system continuos BIT.

The BIT shall detect at least 95% of the system failure rate.

2 Failure Isolation Capability

      i. At least 90% of the detected failure should be isolated to a single
         LRU;

      i. At least 95% of the detected failure should be isolated to two or
         less LRUs, one of which shall be the single failed LRU.

     ii. At least 99% of the detected failure should be isolated to three or
         less LRUs, one of which shall be the single failed LRU

The VENDOR should define in the proposal the failure isolation requirements
at Printed Circuit Board (PCB) level.

The rate of false alarm indication should not exceed 2% of the total
failure indications.


3 Tasks to be undertaken by the Supplier

Compliance with the above testability requirements shall be demonstrated by
analysis in
accordance with MIL-HDBK-217F. This analysis shall be delivered within six
months of receipt of the equipment order. In the event of design changes
following the receipt of the equipment order, the Supplier shall submit
revised documentation within two months of the change being agreed.



   QUALITY ASSURANCE


1 General

Formal tests and demonstration of design characteristics and performance
shall be conducted in accordance with the requirements of this section.


1 Responsibility for Inspection

Unless otherwise specified in the contract or purchase order, the VENDOR
shall be responsible for the performance of the inspection and test
requirements specified herein.

The VENDOR may utilize his own facilities or any commercial laboratory
acceptable to AGUSTA.

At AGUSTA discretion, the tests conducted by the VENDOR may be repeated or
supplemented with inspections to assure that supplies and services conform
to the requirements set forth in this specification.

2 Qualification Tests

The units shall be designed to comply with the environmental requirements
of DO-160D specified in Table 8.

The performance and functional tests to be performed during the
qualification activity are specified in Table 11.

Qualification tests shall be conducted on some samples representative of
production assemblies, and shall be accomplished by examination of design
documentation, simulation, analysis and testing, as necessary.  The VENDOR
shall specify in its test documentation and/or certification program the
quantity of units to be used during testing, as well as the test
requirements applicable to each of them.

1 Failure

Failure of any part, component, or assembly (except for destructive test
failures) specified herein shall be cause for equipment evaluation for
potential redesign.

A part, component, or assembly, is considered to have failed if, after once
having functioned properly, it no longer functions properly.

2 Rejection

Test specimens which fail to meet the requirements specified herein and
have been rejected for any reason may be modified or redesigned to correct
the defects and/or problem.  After appropriate corrective actions have been
taken, all necessary tests shall be repeated.  The VENDOR shall maintain a
traceability of all defects/problems and associated corrective actions and
provide to AGUSTA the problem report for review and approval.


3 Acceptance Tests

Production units submitted for acceptance under contract or purchase order
shall be subjected to the tests specified in Table 11.

1 Rejection and Retest

When a production item fails to meet the requirements of the Acceptance
Test Procedures (ATP), units still on hand or produced after a unit which
fails the ATP shall not be accepted until the extent and cause of failure
are determined.

After appropriate actions have been taken, all necessary tests shall be
repeated.

2 Continuation of Acceptance Tests

Acceptance tests may be continued pending investigation of a test failure.

However, final acceptance of items on hand, or those later produced, shall
not be made until it is determined that they meet all the requirements of
this specification.

3 Corrective Action and Retest

Corrective action and retest shall be required for each failure that can be
attributed to poor manufacture or design practices or that adversely
affects mission completion.  All corrective actions shall be a
responsibility of the VENDOR.

4 Defects in Accepted Items

The investigation of a test failure could indicate that defects may exist
in items already accepted.

If so, the VENDOR shall advise AGUSTA immediately of all defects likely to
be found and methods of correcting them.

4 Environmental Stress Screening (ESS)

An ESS test shall be conducted on each unit furnished under this
specification.  The test shall be conducted after each unit has been
calibrated and sealed.

The test shall consist of a minimum of at least 40 hours of  temperature.
Each cycle shall contain a dwell time of 1 hour at a temperature of -32 °C
and 1 hour at a temperature of +70°C

Primary and lighting power and input signal shall be applied to the unit,
and cycled ON and OFF during each temperature cycle.

The unit shall be vibrated with a 60 Hz sine wave at a 3.0 g ± 0.5 g level
for 15 min. sequential to thermal cycling.

Following the ESS, the unit shall pass the ATP.

The unit shall meet the requirements of this specification during and
following the ESS described above.


The VENDOR may propose alternative but equivalnet test method, such as
Highly Accelerated Stress Screening (HASS), should it be part of standard
VENDOR quality assurance program.


1 ESS Test Failure


Units which fail during the ESS test shall be rejected and removed from the
test at the time of failure.

The cause of the failure shall be determined, the unit repaired and
returned to ESS at the same point in testing where it was removed.  All
test failures (including ESS failures) shall be documented and controlled
by the VENDOR.


5 Test Conditions



1 Standard Conditions


The following conditions shall be used to establish normal performance
characteristics under standard conditions:

a) Atmospheric conditions:
Pressure:   Local ambient
Temperature:     23 ± 5 °C
Humidity:   Ambient (not to exceed 85%)

b)    Primary power:   27.5 ± 0.5 VDC

c)    Lighting power:  0 to 5 VDC


2 Position


Unless otherwise stated, all tests shall be conducted with the units
mounted in their normal operating position (longitudinal axis horizontal).


6 Visual Inspection


The units shall be inspected to determine compliance with this
specification with respect to materials, workmanship, dimensions, weight,
finish, marking and connectors.


2 Documentation Requirements


Unless otherwise specified, the VENDOR shall submit three copies of the
following documents to AGUSTA for approval:


a)    Technical Specification, including, at least, the following:


      1) Internal block diagram

      2) System description (hardware, redundancy concept, power supply,
         lighting, input/output, microprocessor, software, built-in test,
         etc.)


      3) Functional description (normal and degraded modes of operation,
         etc.)


      4) MMI requirements (display formats and contents, display control
         modes, symbology standards, failure indications, etc.)


      5) Performance characteristics (display luminance, color, contrast,
         and resolution, dimming, NVG compatibility, power consumption, data
         accuracy and resolution, etc.)


      6) Physical characteristics (weight, dimensions, mounting, etc.)


      7) MTBF calculation in accordance with MIL-HDBK-217F


      8) Testability and maintainability aspects
         The Technical Specification should be prepared in accordance with
         MIL-STD-490A.


   b) Interface Control Document (ICD), including: electrical and mechanical
      interfaces, installation constraints, etc.


c) Safety Assessment Analysis


   d) Failure Mode and Effects Analysis (FMEA) in accordance with
      MIL-STD-1629A

   e) Software Accomplishment Summary and Plan for Software Aspects of
      Certification in accordance with DO-178B


   f) Qualification Test Plan (QTP), including all the test procedures
      required to obtain the formal qualification from the cognizant
      Authority

   g) Qualification Test Report (QTR), including all test results obtained
      during the formal qualification phase of the IDS

h)          Letter of approval released by the cognizant Authority


      All deliverable documentation shall be written in English Language.


      The International System (SI) of units and dimensions shall be
      included in the documentation.

The minimum identifications required for the documentation are:

   a) Date of issue in the form dd/mm/yy
   b) Subject of the document
   c) Author and signature
   d) Reference
   e) Traceability of text modifications

The nomenclature and terminology used to define all hardware, software and
data items shall be consistent throughout the program.

The VENDOR shall also produce, maintain and submit to AGUSTA a list of
acronyms and abbreviations used in the program documentation, along with
their meanings.


   PREPARATION FOR DELIVERY



1 Packaging


The IDS units be individually packaged according to best commercial
practice, to ensure safe delivery to AGUSTA and to withstand normal
handling and storage.


2 Marking


Each package be durably and legibly marked with the following information,
in such a manner that the marking will not become damaged when the packages
are opened.

   a) Nomenclature
   b) Purchase Order Number
   c) VENDOR Part Number
   d) VENDOR Name or Trademark
   e) AGUSTA Part Number




   Notes



1 Intended Use


The units covered by this specification are intended for use on A109
helicopter.


2 Changes and Deviations


Request for changes or deviations to the requirements of this specification
shall be addressed to AGUSTA Engineering Department.


3 Additional Copies


Additional copies of this specification may be obtained from AGUSTA
Purchasing Department.

      REQUIREMENTS


1     Rounding of Parameter Values



             Rounding of all digital values shall be provided into the IDS,
             as specified in the following examples.


             (a) Rotor Speed (NR) Indication


                  EDU 1 shall display 100% when the input signal is 99.5 (
                  NR < 100.5%.


                  The color change of NR indication and the activation of
                  ROTOR HIGH and ROTOR LOW warning annunciations shall occur
                  as specified below.


                  (1)  Power-on Limits


                 220. ROTOR HIGH  NR ( 105.5% RPM
                 221. ROTOR LOW   NR <   95.5% RPM


                  (2)  Power-off Limits


                 222. ROTOR HIGH  NR ( 110.5% RPM
                 223. ROTOR LOW   NR <   94.5% RPM


             (b) Torque Indication


                  EDU 1 shall display 100% when the input signal is 99.5 (
                  TRQ < 100.5%


                  The color change of Torque indication shall occur as
                  specified below.


               224. AEO Condition from Green to Yellow   TRQ ( 100.5%
               225. AEO Condition from Yellow to Red          TRQ ( 107.0%
               226. OEI Condition from Green to Yellow        TRQ ( 133.5%
               227. OEI Condition from Yellow to Red          TRQ ( 162.5%


                  The activation of XMSN OVTRQ caution annunciation shall
                  occur when the total Torque is as specified below.


                 228. AEO Condition     TRQ 1 + TRQ 2 ( 215% (( 968 SHP)
                 229. OEI Condition     TRQ 1 or TRQ 2 ( 162.5% (( 730 732
                      SHP)



                 2     Analog Scales Requirements



             The following design criteria shall be adopted, as
             appropriate:


             (a) Scale linearity and sensitivity may differ in the most
                 important area, according to the selected screen format.


             (b) Whatever scaling is selected for the green and yellow
                 bands, the same scaling should be included in the red bands
                 just above and below the green and yellow bands, as
                 appropriate (e.g. 1% for N2/NR parameters).


             (c) Resolution should not be so fine as to cause objectionable
                 motion on some pointers (e.g. 0.3% max. for N2/NR
                 parameters).


             (d) If necessary, linearity and sensitivity of some analog
                 scales above the red transient limit mark shall change to
                 allow the pointer to reach the 140th pixel.


                 For instance, the actual change point for N2/NR parameters
                 shall be 1% above the red transient mark to allow for
                 pointer overshoot.


             (e) Scale compression may be required for some parameters.


           NOTE:  The total number of pixel for analog scale and the ranges
                  listed in this section, shall be met but may be exceeded.

4 Alternate TORQUE Computation


When the Torque signal is declared invalid by the EECU (see Table B9), EDU
1 shall revert to the DAU and display the back-up signal provided through
the measurement of N2 probe and keep the bus communication with the
affected EECU for all the other valid parameters.

The following algorithm shall be used for the back-up torque computation:


TRQalt = ((TS - TM)/(TS + TM) - 0.033333( * 1504.8 * SCQGI + SCQBI


             Where:


                  TS:  Torque Space counter (N2 probe)
                  TM:  Torque Mark counter (N2 probe)
                  SCQGI:     Torque Gain Trim value (see Table B2)
                  SCQBI:     Torque Bias Trim value (see Table B2)
                  MOT: Main Oil Temperature


The error on this computation can be up to 7% based on 100% = 394 lb-ft.

EDU 1 shall display a sensor failure annunciation when both the EECU and
DAU Torque inputs are lost.

The cross-monitoring function shall be in accordance with the requirements
of para. 3.3.1.13.

3 Synthetic TOT Computation



When the TOT signal is declared invalid by the EECU, EDU 1 shall revert to
the DAU and display the information provided by the thermocouple harness of
the affected engine, corrected with the algorithm specified as:


For Ng < 40% : MGT = (T6 - T1) + TCOMPENSATION (if T1 is not available),
For 40% < = Ng < = 50%. Progressive transition between both algorithms
For Ng > 50%. The following is the algorithm to be used to process the
             secondary signal :

TaltMGT = TCEGT + TCOMPENSATION + A + B + C + D - TRIM (°C)

Where :

TCEGT = EGT - T1 = Exhaust Gas Temperature Compensated for T1 - input to
airframe system

TCOMPENSATION = TOAT + Tcharge heat (TOAT accuracy should be within ± 3°C
of T1)

Tcharge heat = The temperature rise of the air up to the engine inlet
screen (to be mutually agreed upon by P&WC and the Airframe Manufacture).
Use a value of 5°C.

A = 1150 - (46.604 x Ng) + (0.61333 x Ng²) - (2.2967 x 10-3 x Ng3)

B = 10.5 - (0.72932 x TCOMPENSATION) + (2.0525 x 10-3 x TCOMPENSATION2 )
        - (7.0639 x 10-6 x TCOMPENSATION3).

C = C0 + C1 x Ng + (C2 + C3 x Ng) x Pamb.

Altitude compensation constant : C0 = C1 = C2 = C3 = 0.


             For Ng (  85% = 14.8
D =
             For Ng >  85% = -5.5556 + 10.891 x Ng - 0.22256 x Ng² + 1.1441
x 10-3 x Ng3 .

TRIM = Sampling Correction Factor defined on engine data plate (°C).

Note : If the engine trim is not available, this algorithm is invalid and
should not be used.


The following are the units to be used for the above parameters :

Ng :  %
TCOMPENSATION :  °C
TCEGT :     °C (input to airframe converted to Engineering units by
Airframe Manufacture).
TRIM :      °C
A :   °C
B :   °C
Talt MGT :  °C

The device attached to the compensated Exhaust Gas Temperature output
should have a minimum impedance of 50 Kohms.

However, EDU 1 shall always keep the bus communication with the affected
EECU for all the other valid parameters.


As the thermocouple harness is made of copper-copper wires, no cold
junction compensation shall be provided into the DAU.

EDU 1 shall display a sensor failure annunciation (3 red dashes) when both
the EECU and DAU TOT inputs are lost.

The cross-monitoring function shall be in accordance with the requirements
of para. 3.3.1.13.

   ENGINE  OIL  PRESSURE



1 Display Requirements


             (a) Presentation:    One vertical bar and one digital readout
         per engine
             (b) Range:     0 to 150.5 PSI (analog)
                 0 to 200 PSI (digital)
             (c) Resolution:      1 PSI (digital readout)



         2 Markings


|Color Markings |Range (PSI)    |
|Red band       |Upper Area B   |
|               |(*)            |
|Yellow band    |Upper Area A   |
|               |(*)            |
|Green band     |Variable (*)   |
|Yellow band    |Lower Area A   |
|               |(*)            |
|Red band       |Lower Area B   |
|               |(*)            |


             Note (*):  The boundaries of color markings shall vary
             according to the requirements specified in Figure 10 of this
             report.


             The oil pressure indication shall be corrected for temperature
             effects during engine starting with oil temperature 10 < EOT <
             82°C, as follows:


                  EOPcorr = EOPact + 30.4 * ln (EOT/82)


             For oil temperature 82 < EOT ( 130 °C, the following
             compensation shall apply:


                  EOPcorr = EOPact + ((EOT - 82) * 0.333(


             Where:


                  EOPcorr:    corrected engine oil pressure for temperature
                  EOPact:    actual engine oil pressure
                  EOT: actual engine oil temperature


             The EOPact value shall be displayed in the digital readout,
             while the EOPcorr value shall be associated to the analog
             pointer. The pointer and the digital box shall change the
             color based upon EOPcorr. The EOPact value can reach 200 PSI
             during cold starts.
             In case of an invalid EOT value, the EOP compensation shall
             not be executed (i.e. EOPcorr = EOPact).


             The OIL PRES annunciation shall be displayed when the EOPcorr
             value falls in the upper or lower Area B of Figure 10 for more
             than 5 sec..

|ANNEX B - RS-422A EECU Interface Control     |
|Document                                     |







10. SCOPE

This Annex defines the functional interface requirements of the ECU RS-422A
data output contained in the Pratt & Whitney PW207C Interface Control
Documents ER4901-00.

The IDS shall add the corresponding engine designation number (#1 or #2) to
the specified messages, as appropriate.

Unless otherwise specified, it is assumed that logic level 1 = active.

|Table B3 - ER4901-00- System Status Word 1 (DSCWD 1)                  |
|Bit Number|Description        |IDS Requirement                        |
|1         |Spare              |Do not use                             |
|2         |System Caution     |Do not use. Specific messages are      |
|          |                   |displayed, whereas appropriate.        |
|3         |EECU Critical      |Display the #1 or #2 ECU FAIL warning  |
|          |Failure            |and record the message in the Fault    |
|          |                   |Log.                                   |
|          |                   |The EECU also sets DSCWD1.5 (MAN       |
|          |                   |caution)                               |
|4         |Engine Out         |Display the ENG 1 (2) OUT warning and  |
|          |                   |record the message in the Fault Log.   |
|          |                   |Suppress the indication when PMS = Off |
|          |                   |and WOW = active and N1 <= 50%         |
|5         |Auto/Manual Switch |Display the MAN caution and record the |
|          |in Manual Mode     |message #1 or #2 MAN in the Fault Log  |
|6         |OEI Standby Mode   |Do not use                             |
|          |Active             |                                       |
|7         |PMS = Off          |Display the red dots on TOT scale (see |
|          |                   |3.3.4.4 for details)                   |
|8         |Reserved as Blank  |Do not use (always = 0)                |
|9         |PMS = Idle         |Display the red dots on TOT scale (see |
|          |                   |3.3.4.4 for details).                  |
|          |                   |Display the IDLE advisory on N2 scale  |
|          |                   |and record the message #1 or #2 IDLE in|
|          |                   |the Fault Log if WOW = inactive.       |
|10        |PMS = Fly          |Remove the red dots from TOT scale when|
|          |                   |N1 > 55% (display declutter).  See     |
|          |                   |3.3.4.4  for details.                  |
|11        |N2 Datum at 100%   |Display the cyan 100 legend under NR   |
|          |(NORM)             |digital box                            |
|12        |N2 Datum at 102%   |Display the cyan 102 legend under NR   |
|          |(HIGH)             |digital box                            |
|          |                   |(under NR legend in Reversionary Mode) |
|13        |Overspeed Test     |Disable the N2 exceedance recording and|
|          |Enabled            |display OVSPD TEST caution             |
|14        |Aircraft Power     |Do not use                             |
|          |Available to EECU  |                                       |
|15        |HSS for O/S        |If Bit 13 = 0, display the #1 or #2    |
|          |Solenoid Activated |OVSPD caution and record the message in|
|          |                   |the Fault Log.                         |
|16        |Reserved as Blank  |Do not use (always = 0)                |


|Table B4 - ER4901-00 System Status Word 2 (DSCWD 2)                   |
|Bit Number|Description        |IDS Requirement                        |
|1         |ECU in Manual Mode |Display the MAN caution and record the |
|          |                   |message #1 or #2 MAN in the Fault Log  |
|2         |OEI Training Mode  |When also Bit 3 is set, display the TNG|
|          |Active             |caution and rearrange the N1, TOT and  |
|          |                   |TRQ scales to show derated values      |
|          |                   |Record the number of Training          |
|          |                   |activations in the CUM Log.            |
|3         |OEI Training Switch|When also Bit 2 is set, display the TNG|
|          |Selected to        |caution and rearrange the N1, TOT and  |
|          |Training           |TRQ scales to show derated values      |
|4         |TOT/TRQ Match      |Do not use.                            |
|5         |Training Switch    |Do not use                             |
|          |Relay              |                                       |
|6         |2 ½ min OEI        |Display the OEI symbol in yellow on N1,|
|          |Excursion          |TOT, and TRQ scales.                   |
|7         |2 ½ min OEI Single |Change the color of OEI symbol from    |
|          |Excursion Limit    |yellow to red and blink it             |
|          |Expiration         |                                       |
|8         |Reserved as Blank  |Do not use (always = 0)                |
|9         |Starter/Ignition   |Display the START advisory             |
|          |Activated          |                                       |
|10        |OEI Training Switch|Do not use                             |
|          |Selected to Standby|                                       |
|11        |Power Limiter      |If = 0, display the LIMITER ON         |
|          |Selected           |advisory.                              |
|          |                   |Record the number of LIMITER           |
|          |                   |activations in the CUM Log.            |
|12        |Single Excursion   |Change the status of Red OEI symbol    |
|          |Time Limit at 2.5  |from blink to steady                   |
|          |min OEI power on N2|                                       |
|13        |N1 Corrected Limit |Do not use                             |
|          |Active             |                                       |
|14        |SpareTotal         |Do not useDisplay the #1 or #2 OEI TIME|
|          |Excursion Time     |caution and record the message in the  |
|          |Limit at 2.5 min   |Fault Log                              |
|          |OEI power on N2    |                                       |
|15        |Spare              |Do not use                             |
|16        |Reserved as Blank  |Do not use (always = 0)                |


|Table B5 - ER4901-00 - System Fault Word 1 (NCFUR 1)                      |
|Bit Number|Description          |IDS Requirement                          |
|1         |Power Management     |Display the #1 or #2 PMS caution and     |
|          |Switch (PMS)         |record the message in the Fault Log      |
|          |Interface Failed     |                                         |
|2         |Training Switch      |Record the message TNG SWITCH in the     |
|          |Interface Failed     |Fault Log and display the ECU MAINT      |
|          |                     |caution when PMS = Off and WOW = active  |
|3         |NR Datum Switch      |Display the RPM SELECT caution and record|
|          |Interface Failed     |the message in the Fault Log             |
|4         |TOT/TRQ Match Switch |Record the message TOT/TRQ SWITCH in the |
|          |Interface Failed     |Fault Log and display the ECU MAINT      |
|          |                     |caution when PMS = Off and WOW = active  |
|5         |T6 Sub-system Failed |Display the #1 or #2 TOT LIMITER caution |
|          |                     |and record the message in the Fault Log. |
|          |                     |Revert to DAU for back-up TOT data.      |
|          |                     |If no signal from the DAU, remove the    |
|          |                     |pointer from the scale and display red   |
|          |                     |dashes in white TOT box.                 |
|          |                     |The EECU can also sets DSCWD1.5 (MAN     |
|          |                     |caution) depending upon engine operating |
|          |                     |conditions                               |
|6         |T1 Sensor Failed     |Record message T1 INPUT in Fault Log &   |
|          |                     |display ECU MAINT caution when PMS = Off |
|          |                     |& WOW = active. If NCFUR3 bit 1 also set |
|          |                     |Revert to DAU for back-up TOT data.      |
|          |                     |Invalidate Fuel Prediction (WFPRED word  |
|          |                     |23)                                      |
|7         |Torque Shaft RTD     |Record message #1 or #2 TRQ SHAFT RTD in |
|          |Sensor Failed        |Fault Log & display ECU MAINT caution    |
|          |                     |when PMS = Off & WOW = active            |
|8         |Reserved as Blank    |Do not use (always = 0)                  |
|9         |NR Sensor Failed     |Display the ECU MAINT caution when PMS = |
|          |                     |Off and WOW = active and record the      |
|          |                     |message NR INPUT in the Fault Log. If    |
|          |                     |NCFUR2 Bit 4 and CFUR1 Bit 1 also set,   |
|          |                     |use the NR signal from the other EECU    |
|          |                     |(if available and valid) otherwise,      |
|          |                     |revert to DAU for back-up NR data.       |
|10        |PMA/Low ECU Power    |Record the message #1 or #2 PMA in the   |
|          |Failure              |Fault Log and display the ECU MAINT      |
|          |                     |caution when PMS = Off and WOW = active. |
|11        |Overspeed Solenoid   |Display the OVSPD TEST caution.          |
|          |Discrete Failed      |                                         |






|Table B5 - ER4901-00 - System Fault Word 1 (NCFUR 1)  cont'd              |
|Bit Number|Description          |IDS Requirement                          |
|12        |DCU Failed           |Record message #1 or #2 DCU in the Fault |
|          |                     |Log and display it as caution when PMS = |
|          |                     |Off and WOW = active                     |
|13        |EECU to EECU ARINC   |If NCFUR2 Bit 7=0 display the MGT MATCH  |
|          |429 Interface        |caution and record the message in the    |
|          |(Cross-talk) Failed  |Fault Log.                               |
|          |                     |If NCFUR2 Bit  7=1 display the TRQ       |
|          |                     |LIMITER caution and record the message in|
|          |                     |the Fault Log.                           |
|          |                     |If NCFUR3 Bit 1 also set, revert to DAU  |
|          |                     |for back-up TOT data and invalidate Fuel |
|          |                     |Prediction                               |
|          |                     |(WFPRED word 23).                        |
|          |                     |If NCFUR2 Bit 6 also set, revert to DAU  |
|          |                     |for back-up TRQ and N2 data.             |
|          |                     |If NCFUR2 Bit 14 also set, invalidate    |
|          |                     |Collective Pitch (CLP word 6).           |
|          |                     |If NCFUR2 Bit 15 also set, invalidate    |
|          |                     |Ambient Pressure (PAMB word 18) and Fuel |
|          |                     |prediction                               |
|          |                     |(WFPRED word 23).                        |
|          |                     |If NCFUR2 Bit 4 and CFUR1 Bit 1 also set,|
|          |                     |use the NR signal from the other EECU (if|
|          |                     |available and valid) otherwise, revert to|
|          |                     |DAU for back-up NR data.                 |
|14        |EECU to IDS RS-422   |Do not use.                              |
|          |Interface Failed     |                                         |
|15        |P1 Sensor Failed     |Record message P1 INPUT in the Fault Log |
|          |                     |& display ECU MAINT caution when PMS =   |
|          |                     |Off & WOW = active                       |
|          |                     |If NCFUR2 Bit 15 also set, invalidate    |
|          |                     |Ambient Pressure (PAMB word 18) and Fuel |
|          |                     |prediction                               |
|          |                     |(WFPRED word 23).                        |
|16        |Reserved as Blank    |Do not use (always = 0)                  |


|Table B6 - ER4901-00 - System Fault Word 2 (NCFUR 2)                  |
|Bit Number|Description    |IDS Requirements                            |
|1         |EECU Manual    |Record the message #1 or #2 ECU MAN RELAY in|
|          |Discrete Failed|the Fault Log and display the ECU MAINT     |
|          |               |caution when PMS = Off and WOW = active     |
|2         |ECU FAIL       |Record the message #1 or #2 ECU FAIL RELAY  |
|          |Discrete Failed|in the Fault Log and display the ECU MAINT  |
|          |               |caution when PMS = Off and WOW = active.    |
|          |               |The EECU also sets DSCWD1.3 (ECU FAIL       |
|          |               |warning)                                    |
|3         |Overspeed      |Display the #1 or #2 OVSPD DET caution and  |
|          |Selection      |record the message in the Fault Log         |
|          |Configuration  |                                            |
|          |Fault          |                                            |
|4         |Local NR Fault |Display the ECU MAINT caution when PMS = Off|
|          |               |and WOW = active and record the message #1  |
|          |               |or #2                                       |
|          |               |NR INPUT in the Fault Log.                  |
|          |               |If NCFUR1 Bit 13 and CFUR1 Bit 1 also set,  |
|          |               |or                                          |
|          |               |if NCFUR1 Bit 9 and CFUR1 Bit 1 also set,   |
|          |               |use the NR signal from the other EECU (if   |
|          |               |available and valid) otherwise, revert to   |
|          |               |DAU for back-up NR data.                    |
|5         |LIMITER ON     |Record the message LIMITER SWITCH in the    |
|          |Switch         |Fault Log                                   |
|          |Interface      |The EECU also set DSCWD2.11 (LIMITER ON     |
|          |Failed         |advisory)                                   |
|6         |TRQ/N2-A       |Record the message #1 or #2 TRQ/N2-A INPUT  |
|          |Interface      |in the Fault Log and display the ECU MAINT  |
|          |Failure        |caution when PMS = Off and WOW = active.    |
|          |               |Up to 7% error in TRQ measurement may exist.|
|          |               |                                            |
|          |               |If also TRQ/N2-B input is not valid, the    |
|          |               |EECU also sets CFUR1.1, DSCWD1.3 (ECU FAIL  |
|          |               |warning), DSCWD1.5 (MAN caution) and        |
|          |               |NCFUR2.3 (OVSPD DET caution).               |
|          |               |If NCFUR1 Bit 13 also set, revert to DAU for|
|          |               |back-up TRQ                                 |
|          |               |and N2 data.                                |
|7         |TRQ/N2-B       |If NCFUR1 Bit 13=0 display the #1 or #2 ECU |
|          |Interface      |MAINT caution when PMS = OFF and WOW =      |
|          |Failure        |active. Record the message #1 or #2 TRQ/N2-B|
|          |               |INPUT in the Fault Log.                     |
|          |               |If NCFUR1 Bit  13=1 display the TRQ LIMITER |
|          |               |caution and record the message in the Fault |
|          |               |Log.                                        |
|          |               |The EECU also sets NCFUR2.3 (OVSPD DET      |
|          |               |caution).                                   |
|          |               |If also TRQ/N2-A input is not valid, the    |
|          |               |EECU also sets CFUR1.1, DSCWD1.3 (ECU FAIL  |
|          |               |warning) and DSCWD1.5 (MAN caution).        |
|8         |Reserved as    |Do not use (always = 0)                     |
|          |Blank          |                                            |
|9         |LCF / Creep    |To be used for invalid transmitted word     |
|          |Count Disable  |criteria Table B9 words 26 through 30.      |





|Table B6 - ER4901-00- System Fault Word 2 (NCFUR 2) cont'd            |
|Bit Number|Description    |IDS Requirements                            |
|10        |Collective     |Record the message CP INPUT in the Fault Log|
|          |Pitch Sensor   |and display the ECU MAINT caution when PMS =|
|          |Failed         |Off and WOW = active                        |
|          |               |If NCFUR2 Bit 14 also set, invalidate       |
|          |               |Collective Pitch (CLP word 6).              |
|11        |CP Crosscheck  |Display ECU MAINT caution when PMS = Off and|
|          |Failure        |WOW = active & record the message CP MISCMP |
|          |               |in the Fault Log.                           |
|12        |Pamb Crosscheck|Display ECU MAINT caution when PMS = Off &  |
|          |Failure        |WOW = active and record the message P1      |
|          |               |MISCMP in the Fault Log.                    |
|13        |T1 Crosscheck  |Display ECU MAINT caution when PMS = Off &  |
|          |Failure        |WOW = active & record the message T1 MISCMP |
|          |               |in the Fault Log.                           |
|14        |Local CLP Fault|Record the message #1 or #2 CP INPUT in the |
|          |               |Fault Log and display the ECU MAINT caution |
|          |               |when PMS = Off and WOW = active             |
|          |               |If NCFUR1 Bit 13 also set, or if NCFUR2 Bit |
|          |               |10 also set, invalidate Collective Pitch    |
|          |               |(CLP word 6).                               |
|15        |Local Pamb     |Record message #1 or #2 P1 INPUT in the     |
|          |Fault          |Fault Log & display ECU MAINT caution when  |
|          |               |PMS = Off & WOW = active                    |
|          |               |If NCFUR1 Bit 15 also set, or if NCFUR1 Bit |
|          |               |13 also set, invalidate Ambient Pressure    |
|          |               |(PAMB word 18) and Fuel Prediction (WFPRED  |
|          |               |word 13).                                   |
|16        |Reserved as    |Do not use (always = 0)                     |
|          |Blank          |                                            |



|Table B7 - ER4901-00 - System Fault Word 3 (NCFUR 3)                |
|Bit Number |Description     |IDS Requirements                        |
|1          |Local T1 Fault  |Record message #1 or #2 T1 INPUT in the |
|           |                |Fault Log & display ECU MAINT caution   |
|           |                |when PMS = Off & WOW = active.          |
|           |                |If NCFUR1 Bit 6 also set, or if NCFUR1  |
|           |                |Bit 13 also set, revert to DAU for      |
|           |                |back-up TOT data and invalidate Fuel    |
|           |                |Prediction (WFPRED word 23).            |
|2          |Starter /       |Record the message #1 or #2 STARTER /   |
|           |Ignition  Relay |IGNITION RELAY in the Fault Log and     |

|ANNEX D - ELECTRICAL INTERFACE REQUIREMENTS            |


10. INTRODUCTION


      This Annex D specifies the electrical interface requirements for the
      IDS.


20. APPLICABLE DOCUMENTS


      The applicable documents are listed in section 2.




30. REQUIREMENTS


2 Data Inputs/Outputs



             The following data inputs and outputs shall be accommodated by
             the DAU, as required:


             (a) Inputs


                 (a.1)  +28 VDC/open signal


            230. State 1:  Voltage > 5 VDC (R < 10 Ohms to 28 VDC)
            231. State 0:  Open circuit (R > 1 MOhms)


                 (a.2)  Ground/open signal


            232. State 1:  Voltage < 5 VDC (R < 10 Ohms to ground)
            233. State 0:  Open circuit (R > 1 MOhms).


                        Note:  For the A109S, the minimum current for the
                        engine fuel filter shall be 3 mA when the signal is
                        active (State 1).


                 (a.3)  Null voltage signal


            234. State 1:  Open circuit (R > 1 MOhms)
            235. State 0:  Voltage > 5 VDC (R < 10 Ohms to 28 VDC)


             (b) Outputs


                 (b.1)  Lamp driver


           236. State 1:  +28 VDC, 100 mA
           237. State 0:  Open circuit (R > 1 MOhms)


                  (b.2)  Relay driver (high side switch type)


           238. State 1:  +28 VDC, 50 mA
           239. State 2:  Open circuit (R > 1 MOhms)


                 (b.3)  Relay driver (low side switch type)


           240. State 1:  Voltage < 5 VDC, 50 mA
           241. State 2:  Open circuit (R > 1 Mohms)


             Table D1 shows the electrical characteristics of analog
             input/output signals.


             Table D2 shows the electrical characteristics of input/output
             discrete signals


             Table D3 shows the list of discrete & digital inputs for
             Warning, Caution, Advisory and Status/Maintenance
             Annunciations


             Table D4 shows the DAU pin assignment


             Table D5 shows the EDU pin assignment
